# Reader at Work 1 — Cevap Anahtarı ve Model Yazılar

> Toplam **102** pasaj için tüm Reading, Listening cevap anahtarları, açıklamalar ve Writing model cevapları.

---

## Passage 1: Mary (`raw1_001` · `A1`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **Michigan**

2. **small**

3. **Egypt**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **C) A teacher at METU** — The speaker says Mary Snyder is a teacher at METU.

2. **B) Students come from several different countries.** — The speaker mentions Robab is from Iran, Mohan is from India, and Habib is from Egypt, noting that not all students are from Turkey.

3. **A) India** — The speaker states that Mohan is from India.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — The speaker says the students aren't all from Turkey, and names students from Iran, India, and Egypt.

2. **Not Given** — The passage says Mrs. Snyder is a very good teacher with a small, nice class, but does not mention how long she has taught at METU.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **No, she isn't. She is American.** — The passage states: 'Mary Snyder is American.'

2. **She is from Michigan.** — The passage says: 'She is from Michigan.'

3. **No, she isn't. She is a teacher.** — The passage states: 'She is a teacher at METU.'


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **C) The class includes international students from different countries.** — The passage states that Robab is from Iran, Mohan is from India, and Habib is from Egypt, showing that the class has students from multiple countries.

2. **B) Small and pleasant** — The passage describes her class as 'small, but it is nice', meaning it has a limited number of students and a pleasant atmosphere.

3. **D) To introduce a teacher and her diverse class** — The passage introduces Mary Snyder's nationality, hometown, job, class, and quality as a teacher.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Guided Summary: Introducing Mrs. Snyder):**

Mary Snyder is an American teacher from Michigan. She teaches at METU in Turkey. She is a very good teacher, and her class is small and nice. Her students come from different countries, including Turkey, Iran, India, and Egypt. For example, Robab is from Iran, Mohan is from India, and Habib is from Egypt.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: Class Size and Diversity):**

In my opinion, studying in a small class with international students is very enjoyable. First, in a small class, the teacher can help every student easily. Students can also ask questions without feeling shy. Second, having classmates from different countries is exciting because we can learn about new cultures and practice English together. For example, we can share food and traditions from our countries. In short, a small and international class is a great place to learn.

## Passage 2: Hanako (`raw1_002` · `A1`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **record company / international record company**

2. **Tokyo**

3. **secretary**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **C) She is the director of Pop Fun's Tokyo office.** — The passage says Hanako Sato is the new director of Pop Fun, an international record company with two offices in Japan, and she directs the Tokyo office.

2. **B) Fifteen years** — The speaker states that Mrs. Sato is the first woman to direct the Tokyo office in its fifteen-year history.

3. **D) She studies at New York University.** — The passage says Akiko is 19 and is a student at New York University.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — The passage mentions that Pop Fun has two offices in Japan and that Mrs. Sato directs the Tokyo office.

2. **False** — The passage states that Tomiko (24) and Akiko (19) are both in New York City, not Japan.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **It is an international record company with two offices in Japan.** — The passage states: 'Pop Fun, an international record company with two offices in Japan.'

2. **She works in Tokyo.** — The passage states: 'Mrs. Sato is the first woman to direct the company's Tokyo office...'

3. **Tomiko is 24 and Akiko is 19.** — The passage says: 'Tomiko, 24, is a secretary... Akiko, 19, is a student...'

4. **Tomiko is a secretary for Japan Airlines in New York City, and Akiko is a student at New York University.** — The passage explains their jobs/activities: 'Tomiko, 24, is a secretary for Japan Airlines in New York City. Akiko, 19, is a student at New York University.'


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **A) She has achieved success in both her career and her family life.** — Mrs. Sato is described as a well-known businesswoman, the first woman to direct Pop Fun's Tokyo office, and a successful housewife and mother.

2. **C) Famous** — In the phrase 'a well-known businesswoman', 'well-known' means famous or recognized by many people.

3. **B) Previous directors of the Tokyo office were all men.** — Hanako Sato is the first woman to hold this role in the Tokyo office's 15-year history, which means all previous directors of that office were men.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Guided Summary: Hanako Sato's Life):**

Hanako Sato is a famous businesswoman in Japan. She is the new director of the Tokyo office of Pop Fun, an international record company. She is the first female director there. At home, she is a successful housewife and mother. She and her husband, Taro, have two daughters in New York City. Tomiko is 24 and works as a secretary for Japan Airlines, and Akiko is 19 and studies at New York University.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: Work and Family Balance):**

Yes, I think a person can have both a successful career and a happy family life. First, good time management helps a lot. When people plan their work hours carefully, they can spend quality time with their family in the evenings and on weekends. Second, support from a partner and children is very important. When family members share housework and help each other, nobody feels too tired. Therefore, with good planning and support, people can be successful at work and at home.


---


## Passage 3: Longleat House (`raw1_003` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **140 / one hundred and forty**

2. **antique furniture**

3. **summer**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **B) Between Salisbury and Bath** — The speaker says Longleat House is located 140 kilometres west of London, between Salisbury and Bath.

2. **C) Drive through and see many animals** — The speaker states that visitors can drive through the Safari Park and see many animals there.

3. **A) 8 hours** — The speaker states that Longleat House is open every day from 10 o'clock in the morning to 6 o'clock in the evening.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — The speaker says Longleat House is open every day, whereas the Safari Park is open only in the summer.

2. **Not Given** — The passage mentions that visitors can walk through the house and drive through the Safari Park, but does not mention ticket prices.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **It is 140 kilometres west of London, between the cities of Salisbury and Bath.** — The first sentence states: 'Longleat House is 140 kilometres west of London, between the cities of Salisbury and Bath.'

2. **Lord Bath owns the house.** — The passage states: 'The house is the home of Lord Bath.'

3. **Visitors can see the beautiful rooms and the antique furniture in the rooms.** — The passage says: 'Visitors can walk through and look at the beautiful rooms and they can see the antique furniture in the rooms.'

4. **Visitors can drive through the park and see many animals there.** — The passage states: 'Visitors can drive through the park and see many animals there.'

5. **It is open every day from 10 o'clock in the morning to 6 o'clock in the evening.** — The second paragraph states: 'Longleat House is open every day from 10 o'clock in the morning to 6 o'clock in the evening.'

6. **It is open only in the summer.** — The final sentence states: 'The Safari Park is open only in the summer.'

7. **The house / Longleat House** — In the second and third sentences ('The house is the home of Lord Bath. It is about 400 years old.'), 'It' refers back to 'The house' (Longleat House).

8. **Visitors** — In the sentence 'Visitors can walk through and look at the beautiful rooms and they can see the antique furniture in the rooms', 'they' refers to 'Visitors'.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **D) To give visitor information about a historic house and park** — The passage explains where Longleat House is, what visitors can see and do there, and its opening hours, serving as an overview for visitors.

2. **B) Very old** — Longleat House is about 400 years old, and visitors see 'antique furniture' in its rooms, meaning very old and valuable furniture.

3. **C) A tourist visiting in January can see the house, but not the Safari Park.** — The Safari Park is open only in the summer, whereas Longleat House is open every day, so in winter a visitor can tour the house but not the Safari Park.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Guided Summary: Visiting Longleat House):**

Longleat House is a 400-year-old historic home owned by Lord Bath, located 140 kilometres west of London between Salisbury and Bath. Tourists can walk inside to admire its beautiful rooms and antique furniture. They can also drive through the Safari Park to watch many animals. While the house is open daily from 10 a.m. to 6 p.m., the Safari Park is open only during summer.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: Visiting Historic Places):**

I would like to visit Longleat House, especially because it offers two different experiences in one place. First, I enjoy history, so walking through a 400-year-old building and seeing antique furniture sounds very interesting. It helps visitors imagine how people lived centuries ago. Second, driving through the Safari Park in summer would be exciting because you can see wild animals up close. Combining history and nature makes Longleat House a wonderful place for a day trip.

## Passage 4: New York University (`raw1_004` · `A1`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **post office**

2. **5th Avenue**

3. **popular**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **B) In Greenwich Village** — The speaker states that New York University is in Greenwich Village.

2. **C) Three** — The speaker says there is a bank on 5th Avenue and two on 8th Street, which makes three banks in total.

3. **A) They are very popular.** — The speaker says the restaurants and coffee shops in Greenwich Village are very popular.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — The passage states that students can get almost everything they need near the university campus.

2. **Not Given** — The passage mentions that there is a post office on 9th Street, but does not mention its opening hours.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **It is in Greenwich Village.** — The first sentence states: 'New York University is in Greenwich Village.'

2. **There is a post office on 9th Street.** — The passage says: 'There is a post office on 9th Street.'

3. **There are three banks (one on 5th Avenue and two on 8th Street).** — The passage states: 'There is a bank on 5th Avenue, and there are two on 8th Street.' (1 + 2 = 3 banks).


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **A) The area around New York University is very convenient for students.** — The passage explains that students can get almost everything they need nearby, including a post office, three banks, restaurants, and coffee shops.

2. **D) Two banks** — In 'there are two on 8th Street', the word 'two' replaces 'two banks' from the first half of the sentence ('There is a bank on 5th Avenue, and there are two on 8th Street.').

3. **C) Students do not need to go far for daily services and food.** — Because there is a post office, three banks, and popular restaurants and coffee shops nearby, we can infer it is a lively and well-served neighborhood.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Guided Summary: Around NYU Campus):**

New York University is located in Greenwich Village. The area around the campus is very convenient for students because they can find almost everything they need nearby. For example, there is a post office on 9th Street and three banks on 5th Avenue and 8th Street. In addition, Greenwich Village has very popular restaurants and coffee shops.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: Campus Facilities):**

In my opinion, having shops, banks, and cafes near a university campus is very helpful for students. First, students are often busy with classes and homework, so they do not have much time to travel far. When a post office or a bank is close to campus, they can finish their tasks quickly between lessons. Second, popular coffee shops and restaurants give students a nice place to relax, eat, or study with friends. Overall, nearby services make student life much easier.


---


## Passage 5: University of London (`raw1_005` · `A1`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **1836**

2. **British Museum**

3. **cheap**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **A) In Bloomsbury** — The speaker begins by saying that the University of London is in Bloomsbury.

2. **A) Things to eat** — The speaker states that there are many restaurants and coffee shops where you can buy things to eat.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — The speaker states that the British Museum is near the university.

2. **Not Given** — The passage only mentions that the university was founded in 1836; it does not say whether it is the oldest in England.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **In Bloomsbury.** — The passage states, 'The University of London is in Bloomsbury.'

2. **The British Museum.** — The passage states, 'The British Museum, which is near the university, is one of these buildings.'

3. **At the many restaurants and coffee shops.** — The passage says, 'There are also many restaurants and coffee shops, where you can buy things to eat.'


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **A) In 1836** — The passage states, 'It was founded in 1836.'

2. **A) They offer good value for their price.** — The passage states that 'the coffee shops are excellent for the price.'

3. **A) well-known** — 'Famous' means known by many people, or well-known.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Summarizing Bloomsbury):**

The University of London is located in Bloomsbury and was founded in 1836. Bloomsbury has many famous buildings, including the British Museum near the university. There are also many restaurants and coffee shops in the area. Although the restaurants are not always cheap, the coffee shops are great for their price.


**Task 2 (A Famous Area in My City):**

A famous place in my city is Taksim Square in Istanbul. Many tourists and students visit it every day. Around the square, there are old buildings, shops, and cultural centers. There are also many restaurants and small cafes on the side streets. Some restaurants are expensive, but you can easily find cheap street food and good tea shops nearby. It is always lively and interesting to walk there.


---


## Passage 6: Jim (`raw1_006` · `A1`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **fifty-two / 52**

2. **American**

3. **Arts**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **A) A famous cameraman** — The speaker introduces Jim Chapman as 'a famous cameraman' who is the new assistant director of Video Sound.

2. **A) Her friend Paul** — The speaker states that Mary 'lives with her friend Paul in London.'


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — The speaker explicitly says, 'Mr. Chapman is not married.'

2. **Not Given** — The passage mentions that Paul lives with Mary in London, but it gives no information about what Paul studies or where he goes.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **It is a large film company.** — The passage calls Video Sound 'a large film company' with fifty-two offices all over the world.

2. **Paris.** — The passage says Mr. Chapman works at the company's Paris office.

3. **Her friend Paul.** — The passage states, 'She lives with her friend Paul in London.'

4. **She is a student at the Faculty of Arts at the University of London.** — The passage says, 'She goes to the Faculty of Arts at the University of London.'

5. **Five (Arts, Science, Engineering, Medicine, and Music).** — The passage mentions the Faculty of Arts as well as the Faculties of Science, Engineering, Medicine, and Music.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **A) Assistant director** — The passage states that Jim Chapman 'is the new assistant director of Video Sound.'

2. **A) He is the first American to work there in thirty years.** — The passage says, 'Mr. Chapman is the first American to work at the company's Paris office in its thirty-year history.'

3. **A) Five** — The passage mentions the Faculties of Arts, Science, Engineering, Medicine, and Music—a total of five faculties.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Introducing Jim and Mary):**

Jim Chapman is a famous American cameraman and the new assistant director of Video Sound, a large film company with fifty-two offices worldwide. He works at its Paris office and is not married. His sister Mary lives in London with her friend Paul and studies at the Faculty of Arts at the University of London.


**Task 2 (Someone I Know):**

My cousin Emre works as a software designer for a technology company in Ankara. The company has offices in three different cities. Emre is not married, and he lives in a small apartment near his office. He also has a younger sister named Zeynep. She lives in Izmir and studies engineering at a large university. Both of them are very busy, so they talk on the phone every weekend.


---


## Passage 7: Kate (`raw1_007` · `A1`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **Australian**

2. **north**

3. **unemployed**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **A) Near Leeds** — Kate says, 'I live near Leeds, in the north-east of England.'

2. **A) Two** — Kate says Bianca is 'married with two children, a girl and a boy.'


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — Kate says of the dentist, 'He's very nice and he's a very good dentist.'

2. **Not Given** — The passage only says George does not have a job and is unemployed; it does not mention what job he is looking for.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **'Kate'.** — Catherine says, 'I'm called "Kate" by my friends.'

2. **Near Leeds, in the north-east of England.** — She states, 'I live near Leeds, in the north-east of England.'

3. **No, she lives near Leeds, while her sister lives near London.** — Catherine lives near Leeds, whereas her sister Bianca lives in Enfield, near London.

4. **She is a dental nurse.** — Catherine says, 'I'm a dental nurse.'

5. **No, she doesn't like it very much.** — She says, 'I don't like my job very much.'

6. **Australia.** — She works for 'a young Australian dentist.'

7. **Yes, she says he is very nice and a very good dentist.** — Catherine says, 'He's very nice and he's a very good dentist.'

8. **Bianca.** — She states, 'My sister is called Bianca.'

9. **Yes, she is.** — The passage says, 'She's married with two children.'

10. **She is a teacher.** — The passage states, 'She's a teacher.'

11. **In a big school in the north of London.** — The passage states, 'She teaches in a big school in the north of London.'

12. **Yes, she likes it very much.** — The passage states, 'She likes her job very much.'

13. **No, she lives near London, in a town called Enfield.** — The passage states she 'lives in a house near London, in a town called Enfield.'

14. **She lives in a house.** — The passage explicitly says she 'lives in a house near London.'

15. **He doesn't have a job; he is unemployed.** — The passage says, 'He doesn't have a job. He's unemployed.'

16. **George.** — The passage says, 'My brother's name is George.'


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **A) Bianca has a daughter and a son.** — The passage states that Bianca has 'two children, a girl and a boy.'

2. **A) Bianca likes her job very much, while Kate does not like hers very much.** — Kate says 'I don't like my job very much', whereas Bianca 'likes her job very much.'

3. **A) without a job** — The passage says, 'He doesn't have a job. He's unemployed.'


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Kate and Her Family):**

Catherine, also called Kate, lives near Leeds and works as a dental nurse for a nice Australian dentist, though she does not like her job very much. Her sister Bianca is a married teacher with two children. She lives in a house in Enfield near London and loves her teaching job. Kate's brother, George, is unemployed.


**Task 2 (My Family and Their Jobs):**

There are four people in my family: my parents, my brother, and me. My father is an engineer and works in a large factory. He likes his job because he enjoys solving problems. My mother is a nurse at a local hospital, and she helps many patients every day. My older brother is a university student in Ankara, and he wants to become a teacher. We all enjoy spending time together during holidays.

## Passage 8: The Statue of Liberty & The Old England Restaurant (`raw1_008` · `A1`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **French**

2. **Italy**

3. **music**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **A) Battery Park** — The speaker says there are ferryboats from Battery Park to Liberty Island.

2. **A) From 6:30 p.m. until midnight** — The advertisement states that the restaurant is open for dinner from 6:30 p.m. until midnight.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — The speaker states that 'The last boat returns at 5:00 p.m.'

2. **Not Given** — The advertisement gives lunch and dinner hours, but it does not mention which days of the week the restaurant is open or closed.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **False** — The statue is on Liberty Island, and visitors go there by ferryboat from Battery Park.

2. **True** — Boats leave Battery Park seven days a week.

3. **False** — The statue is open until 5 pm (3:30 pm is when the last boat leaves Battery Park).

4. **True** — The advertisement says, 'Sit in our lovely garden and try our Italian spaghetti and tomato soup.'

5. **True** — The advertisement warns customers, 'We are very busy at lunch-times!'

6. **Because they are never cold and always delicious.** — The advertisement states, 'Our meals are fantastic. They are never cold and always delicious.'

7. **You can listen to music and dance (and enjoy interesting drinks from different countries).** — The text says, 'Listen to music and dance from 11 pm to 3 am. We serve interesting drinks from different countries.'


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **A) 15 minutes** — The first passage states, 'The trip to the Statue of Liberty takes 15 minutes.'

2. **A) It is free.** — The passage states that tickets are 'free for children under 3 years old.'

3. **A) To advertise the restaurant and invite customers to visit** — The text promotes the restaurant's food, garden, nightclub, and hours, and urges readers to 'Phone NOW!' to book a table.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Visitor Information Summary):**

The Statue of Liberty on Liberty Island was a gift from France to America. Ferryboats from Battery Park run every half hour from 9:30 am to 3:30 pm, and the 15-minute trip costs $6 for adults and $3 for children aged 3-7. Meanwhile, the Old England Restaurant near Bristol offers international meals, a lovely garden, and a nightclub open from 11 pm to 3 am.


**Task 2 (My Favorite Place to Visit or Eat):**

A great place to eat in my town is Deniz Cafe near the sea. It is open every day from 9 am until 11 pm. The cafe has a bright garden with many trees and serves delicious Turkish breakfast, fresh salads, and homemade pasta. The prices are reasonable, and the staff are very friendly. It is a wonderful place to meet friends for lunch or a quiet dinner.

## Passage 9: Tomiko (`raw1_009` · `A1`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **accountant**

2. **Sunday**

3. **twelve-twenty / 12:20**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **C) She dislikes driving in London traffic** — The passage says Tomiko takes the bus because she does not like driving in London traffic.

2. **B) Twenty minutes** — She eats a quick lunch at twelve o'clock and returns by twelve-twenty.

3. **D) She watches TV or listens to music** — After getting home around five, she changes her clothes and watches TV or listens to music.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — The passage states that she finished college last year and is twenty-three years old.

2. **False** — She stops work at four o'clock and gets home around five o'clock.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **They live in Glasgow, far from London.** — The passage states: "Her parents live in Glasgow far from London."

2. **Yes, it is.** — The passage states: "This is Tomiko's first job".

3. **She phones them every Sunday.** — The passage states: "she calls her parents every Sunday."

4. **Because they think she is very young and cannot live alone.** — The passage says: "Tomiko's parents think she's very young and she can't live alone".

5. **By eight o'clock.** — The passage states: "Tomiko has to be at work by eight o'clock."

6. **She goes to work by bus.** — The passage states: "She goes to work by bus".

7. **Because she doesn't like to drive in the London traffic.** — The passage says: "She goes to work by bus, because she doesn't like to drive in the London traffic."

8. **Yes, she does. She is a good worker and very serious.** — The passage describes her as "a good worker" who is "very serious" and takes only a quick twenty-minute lunch.

9. **At four o'clock.** — The passage states: "She stops work at four o'clock."

10. **She changes her clothes and watches TV or listens to music.** — The passage says: "She gets home around five o'clock, changes her clothes and watches TV, or listens to music."


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **B) She feels confident and happy living on her own.** — Tomiko laughs at the idea that she is too young to live alone and reassures her parents that she is happy and everything will be fine.

2. **C) About one hour** — She stops work at four o'clock and arrives home around five o'clock, which takes about one hour.

3. **A) Tomiko's new job and daily routine in London** — The passage describes Tomiko's family background, her job in London, her commute, and her daily routine at work and at home.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Summary of Tomiko's Routine):**

Tomiko is a twenty-three-year-old accountant in London. It is her first job after college. Her parents live in Glasgow and worry about her living alone, but Tomiko is happy. She starts work at eight o'clock and takes the bus because of the traffic. After a quick lunch, she finishes work at four and relaxes at home.


**Task 2 (My Daily Routine):**

My daily routine is busy but enjoyable. On weekdays, I wake up at seven o'clock and have a quick breakfast. I go to school by bus because the traffic is heavy in the morning. My classes start at eight-thirty and finish at three o'clock. When I get home, I change my clothes and rest for a while. In the evening, I study, listen to music, and call my family.

## Passage 10: Tomiko, King and Ted (`raw1_010` · `A1`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **three / 3**

2. **apartment**

3. **accountant**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **A) She hides her fear at night from her parents** — The passage says Tomiko never tells her parents that she sometimes feels afraid at night.

2. **C) At strangers he does not know** — King is friendly, but he barks at people he does not know.

3. **B) He is afraid of King and thinks King may bite him** — Ted is afraid of dogs and does not trust King because King is very big and barks too much.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — The passage states that Tomiko likes London because there are many places to go and a lot of things to do.

2. **False** — The passage explicitly says Tomiko does not have many friends in London.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **Because there are many places to go and a lot of things to do.** — The passage says: "Tomiko likes London. There are many places to go and a lot of things to do".

2. **No, she doesn't. She never tells them that she sometimes feels afraid at night.** — The passage says: "Tomiko never tells her parents, but sometimes she feels afraid at night."

3. **Sometimes at night.** — The passage states: "sometimes she feels afraid at night."

4. **Because she has three locks on her apartment door and a big dog that protects the apartment.** — The passage notes: "Tomiko has three locks on her apartment door and she owns a big dog" and "He also protects Tomiko's apartment when she's at work."

5. **He protects Tomiko and Tomiko's apartment.** — The passage says: "he protects Tomiko. He also protects Tomiko's apartment when she's at work."

6. **He barks at people he doesn't know.** — The passage says: "he barks at people he doesn't know."

7. **Most people feel afraid of King when they hear and see him.** — The passage says: "Most people are afraid of King when they hear and see him."

8. **No, she doesn't have many friends in London.** — The passage states: "Tomiko doesn't have many friends in London".

9. **He works in the same company as Tomiko.** — The passage says: "He works in the same company and he is also an accountant."

10. **Because he is afraid of dogs and thinks King is very big, barks too much, and may bite him someday.** — The passage explains: "He's afraid of dogs... Ted doesn't trust King. He's very big and barks too much. Ted's afraid that King may bite him someday."


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **C) Because she does not want them to worry about her living alone** — The passage mentions Tomiko never tells her parents she feels afraid at night, likely so she does not worry them after telling them everything is fine.

2. **A) Believe that King is safe and will not bite him** — In this context, "trust" means to believe that King is safe and will not hurt or bite him.

3. **D) He is an accountant at Tomiko's company.** — The text explains that Ted works in the same company as Tomiko and is also an accountant.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Tomiko, King, and Ted Summary):**

Tomiko likes London, though she sometimes feels afraid at night. To stay safe, she has three locks on her door and a big dog named King. King protects her and her apartment, and he barks at strangers. Tomiko's boyfriend, Ted, is also an accountant at her company, but he is afraid of dogs and does not trust King.


**Task 2 (Living in a Big City):**

Living in a big city has both advantages and difficulties. There are many interesting places to visit, good restaurants, and job opportunities. However, life in a big city can be noisy and lonely, especially when you are far from your family. Sometimes people feel unsafe at night in a large city. For this reason, having a good friend or a pet can help people feel happier and safer.


---


## Passage 11: The Garston Family (`raw1_011` · `A1`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **village**

2. **meat**

3. **six / 6**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **B) The village shops do not sell dog food** — The passage explains that the shops in the village do not sell dog food, so Mr. Garston buys it in London.

2. **C) The neighbours and the postman** — Both the neighbours (who have a cat) and the postman dislike Bruce and Spot.

3. **A) They go for a long evening walk with him** — The dogs wait by the door, bark, and feel happy because they go for a long walk in the evening with Mr. Garston.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — They live in a small village near London, and Mr. Garston goes to London every day for work.

2. **Not Given** — The passage mentions that Mr. Garston works in London, but it does not say whether Mrs. Garston has a job.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **No, they don't. They live in a small village near London.** — The passage states: "Mr. and Mrs. Garston live in a small village near London."

2. **They eat meat, biscuits and special dog food.** — The passage says: "The dogs eat meat, biscuits and special dog food."

3. **Because the shops in their village don't sell dog food.** — The passage explains: "Mr. Garston buys their food in London because the shops in their village don't sell dog food."

4. **Because the neighbours have got a cat.** — The passage states: "The Garstons have got neighbours, too, but they don't like Bruce and Spot because they've got a cat."

5. **He comes home at six.** — The passage says: "He comes home at six."

6. **Because they go for a long walk in the evening with Mr. Garston.** — The passage states: "They are happy because they go for a long walk in the evening with Mr. Garston."

7. **the Garstons' neighbours** — The sentence contrasts the Garstons with their neighbours, who own a cat and therefore dislike the dogs.

8. **London** — Mr. Garston goes to London every day because he works in London ("there").

9. **the dogs (Bruce and Spot)** — "They" continues the subject from the previous sentences about the dogs waiting near the door and barking.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **D) He works in London and the village shops do not sell it.** — Mr. Garston works in London and comes home at six, and the village shops do not sell dog food, so he buys it in London where he already goes daily.

2. **B) Both the neighbours and the postman have a negative feeling toward the dogs.** — Postmen are often wary of dogs that bark near doors, and the passage notes the neighbours dislike the dogs because they have a cat, while the postman also dislikes them.

3. **A) The Garston family and their two dogs** — The passage introduces Mr. and Mrs. Garston, their two dogs, their neighbours, and Mr. Garston's daily routine with the dogs.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Summary of The Garston Family):**

Mr. and Mrs. Garston live in a small village near London with their two dogs, Bruce and Spot. Mr. Garston works in London and buys dog food there because the village shops do not sell it. Their neighbours, who have a cat, and the postman dislike the dogs. Every evening at six, the dogs wait excitedly for Mr. Garston to take them for a long walk.


**Task 2 (Having Pets at Home):**

I think having a pet is wonderful, especially a dog. Dogs are friendly companions and help people stay active because they need daily walks. Taking care of a pet also teaches responsibility. However, pets need time, food, and space, so owners must look after them well and be respectful to neighbours who do not like animals. Overall, a pet brings a lot of happiness to a home.


---


## Passage 12: Cinemas (`raw1_012` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **14 / fourteen**

2. **tomorrow**

3. **Alice Walker**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **C) Marie** — Marie is the only film in the listings without a 14-year minimum age requirement.

2. **A) Claude Lelouch** — Long Live Life is directed by Claude Lelouch and stars Charlotte Rampling and Michel Piccoli.

3. **D) Three times** — The Color Purple is showing at Cinema West at 3:45, 6:30, and 9:15 (three times).


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — The listing for Cinema 2 says "Tomorrow!", whereas the other cinemas list films for today.

2. **True** — The listing for The Color Purple describes it as "a great critical and public success at Cinema West" and mentions Alice Walker is a Pulitzer prizewinner.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **You can only see Marie.** — Long Live Life, Separate Rooms, and The Color Purple all have a minimum age of 14 years. Only Marie has no age restriction listed.

2. **Four times (at 3.00, 5.10, 7.20, and 9.30).** — Marie has four showtimes listed at Cinema Akun: 3.00, 5.10, 7.20, and 9.30.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **B) Long Live Life** — Only Long Live Life has five showtimes (2:00, 4:00, 6:00, 8:00, and 10:00). Separate Rooms and Marie have four, and The Color Purple has three.

2. **D) Marie** — The advertisement calls it "A true story directed by Roger Donaldson", distinguishing it as based on real events.

3. **A) Widely praised and admired** — In an advertisement praising a film maker, "applauded" means widely admired and praised.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Cinema Listings Overview):**

These cinema listings show four films at different theaters. Today, viewers can watch Long Live Life at Cinema 1, Marie at Cinema Akun, or The Color Purple at Cinema West. Tomorrow, Separate Rooms plays at Cinema 2. Three of the films have a minimum age of 14 years, while Marie is open to younger viewers as well.


**Task 2 (Choosing a Film):**

I would like to see The Color Purple at Cinema West at 6:30. The advertisement describes it as a moving romance and a great critical and public success, which makes it sound very interesting. Also, it is based on a book by a Pulitzer prizewinner, so the story should be strong and meaningful. Watching a film at 6:30 in the evening is also a convenient time after school or work.


---


## Passage 13: Plaza Shopping Centre: A Shopping Experience Without Equal (`raw1_013` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **Harlow**

2. **12,000 / twelve thousand**

3. **Saturday**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **C) By boat** — The text says Plaza Shopping is easy to reach by bus, train, or car, and does not mention travelling by boat.

2. **B) Two independent ramps for entrances and exits** — The text states there are two independent ramps for entrances and exits to make access easier.

3. **D) The Picnic department and the car park** — While the main shopping centre is open Monday to Saturday, the Picnic department and the car park are also open on Sundays.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — Parking at Plaza Shopping is explicitly described as free parking.

2. **True** — The passage mentions that Plaza Shopping has 35,000 square metres of parking space on 3 floors and states there is no longer a need to go into London for shopping.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **False** — The passage states that Plaza Shopping is in the centre of Harlow, and that people no longer need to go into London to shop.

2. **False** — The passage says it is easy to reach by bus, train, or car.

3. **True** — The car park has 3 floors with 35,000 square metres of space and room for 12,000 cars.

4. **False** — The centre's opening hours are 10 am to 10 pm, Monday to Saturday, not 24 hours a day.

5. **True** — The centre is open Monday to Saturday, and the Picnic department is open on Sundays as well.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **A) To persuade local shoppers to visit Plaza Shopping Centre instead of going to London** — The passage highlights the convenient location, free parking, long opening hours, and wide variety to persuade people to shop at Plaza Shopping.

2. **C) People in Harlow previously had to travel to London for major shopping trips** — Saying 'there is no need to go into London for shopping any longer' implies residents previously travelled to London to find everything they wanted.

3. **B) Separate from each other** — In the context of entrances and exits, 'independent' means separate, so entering and exiting cars use different ramps.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Shopping Centre Summary):**

Plaza Shopping Centre is situated in central Harlow right beside the South Coach Station, making it accessible by bus, train, or car. It offers free parking on three floors for up to 12,000 cars, with separate entrance and exit ramps. The centre operates from 10 am to 10 pm Monday through Saturday, while its Picnic department and car park also remain open on Sundays.


**Task 2 (Large Shopping Centres vs. Small Shops):**

In my opinion, large shopping centres are very convenient for busy people. First, they save time because visitors can find clothes, food, and everyday items in a single place without travelling to a distant city centre. Second, features like free parking and easy bus or train connections make shopping much less stressful for families. Although small local shops offer personal service, a large shopping centre makes weekly errands faster and more comfortable for most residents.


---


## Passage 14: The International Hotel (`raw1_014` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **Sauna / sauna**

2. **midnight**

3. **Sunday**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **C) 6:30 p.m. to 10:30 p.m.** — The International Restaurant serves dinner from 6:30 p.m. to 10:30 p.m., while lunch is 12:30 to 2:30 p.m.

2. **A) The First Floor** — The directory lists the Gift Shop and the Boutique on the First Floor.

3. **D) At the Exchange Bureau** — The Exchange Bureau allows guests to exchange foreign currency and traveller's cheques.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — The Coffee Shop opens at 8 a.m. and stays open until midnight every day, whereas the restaurant closes at 10:30 p.m. and the Swimming Pool closes at 9 p.m.

2. **False** — The audio states that Max's Hairdressing and Beauty Salon is open to both men and women.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **Six days a week (Monday to Saturday).** — The Exchange Bureau states 'Closed on Sunday', which means it is open the other six days of the week.

2. **Cannot be determined from the text alone (the original visual floor-plan diagram is incomplete).** — The source page contains a visual floor-plan map whose full spatial layout was not preserved in the text, so what sits directly opposite the Coffee Shop cannot be confirmed from the text alone.

3. **At Max's Hairdressing and Beauty Salon.** — The hotel guide includes 'Max's Hairdressing and Beauty Salon. Have your hair done by Max.'

4. **6:30 pm.** — The International Restaurant listing states '6.30 pm - 10.30 pm for dinner.'

5. **The First Floor (to the Gift Shop).** — The Gift Shop sells stamps, and the Floor Directory places the Gift Shop on the First Floor.

6. **At the Coffee Shop.** — The International Restaurant stops serving lunch at 2:30 pm and does not open for dinner until 6:30 pm, while the Coffee Shop serves snacks and meals from 8 am to midnight every day.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **B) The Coffee Shop** — The Coffee Shop is open from 8 am to midnight every day, which covers 16 hours daily, longer than the bar (14 hours) and the pool (14 hours).

2. **C) Guests who are staying at the hotel** — 'Residents' in a hotel guide refers to guests who are staying in the hotel, who get to use the sauna for free.

3. **D) The Swimming Pool and The International Restaurant** — The restaurant opens at 7 am for breakfast, and the Swimming Pool also opens at 7 am, whereas the Coffee Shop opens at 8 am and the Flower Shop opens at 9 am.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Hotel Facilities Guide):**

The International Hotel offers convenient dining, shopping, and leisure services for guests. Visitors can eat at the Coffee Shop from 8 am to midnight daily or enjoy scheduled meals at The International Restaurant. The hotel also includes a bar, a swimming pool, a free sauna for residents, and Max's salon. In addition, guests can use the Boutique, Gift Shop, Flower Shop, Car Park, and Exchange Bureau, though the Flower Shop and Exchange Bureau close on Sundays.


**Task 2 (Important Hotel Facilities):**

When I choose a hotel, the most important facilities for me are a good restaurant and safe parking. After a long trip, it is very comfortable to have breakfast or dinner inside the hotel without searching for a place to eat outside. In addition, a free or secure car park makes travelling much easier if I arrive by car. While a swimming pool or gift shop is nice to have, good food and safety matter most for a relaxing stay.


---


## Passage 15: Job Advertisements (`raw1_015` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **theatre**

2. **children**

3. **negotiable**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **C) Ages 18 to 30** — The reporter position requires applicants aged 18 to 30, a driving licence, typing, and an interest in sport or theatre.

2. **B) Send handwritten details to Mr. B.K. Graves** — Applicants aged 16 to 19 for the Office Assistant role should send handwritten details to Mr. B.K. Graves.

3. **A) Reporter and Electronic Engineer** — Both the Reporter and the Electronic Engineer advertisements state that a driving licence is necessary.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — The school bus driver advertisement states working hours are 7:00 a.m. to 2:00 p.m. only.

2. **Not Given** — The Electronic Engineer advertisement says 'Salary negotiable', and none of the advertisements state exact pay amounts.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **False** — The Selton Times wants a reporter aged 18-30. A 16-year-old does not meet the age requirement (though a 16-year-old could apply for the Office Assistant job at H.G. White).

2. **False** — The school bus driver works from 7.00 am to 2.00 pm only, so they finish in the early afternoon, not late in the evening.

3. **True** — The Electronic Engineer advertisement lists the address as P.K. Electronics, 41 Stoughton House, Suffolk.

4. **True** — The Office Assistant advertisement tells applicants to send handwritten details to Mr. B.K. Graves at H.G. White.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **D) Good typing and a nice personality** — The Office Assistant job asks for 'a good telephone manner, good typing, nice personality and lots of common sense', not a driving licence or micro-computer experience.

2. **B) The pay can be discussed and agreed upon** — 'Salary negotiable' means the pay is not fixed in advance and can be discussed and agreed upon between the employer and candidate.

3. **C) Reporter for The Selton Times** — A 22-year-old with typing skills, a driving licence, and an interest in sport matches all requirements for the Reporter role (aged 18-30), whereas 22 is too old for Office Assistant (16-19) and too young for School Bus Driver (30-50).


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Job Advertisements Summary):**

The four advertisements offer different jobs with specific requirements. The Selton Times needs a reporter aged 18 to 30 who can type, drive, and enjoys sport or theatre. A school bus driver aged 30 to 50 is wanted from 7 am to 2 pm and must be good with children. H.G. White seeks an office assistant aged 16 to 19 with good typing and a nice personality. Finally, P.K. Electronics needs an electronic engineer with micro-computer experience and a driving licence.


**Task 2 (Choosing a Job):**

If I could choose one of these four jobs, I would apply for the Office Assistant position at H.G. White. First, the job is described as varied and interesting, so every day at work would feel different. Second, I enjoy talking to people politely on the phone and working neatly on a computer. Since the role asks for common sense and a nice personality rather than long technical experience, it is a great opportunity for a young person starting a career.


---


## Passage 16: Young Love at The Cave (`raw1_016` · `A1`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **music**

2. **discos**

3. **four pounds / 4 pounds**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **B) She is a secretary** — Sarah Jones is a secretary, Pete drives a taxi, and Bette teaches music.

2. **C) From 9:00 to 5:00** — Sarah says her office opens at 9:00 and she usually works from 9:00 to 5:00.

3. **A) At 8:30** — The announcement states Young Love are playing at The Cave on Lower Harrington Street at 8:30.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — Sarah, Pete, and Bette work regular jobs in Manchester Monday to Friday and sing in a band at weekends, so the passage says they have got two jobs and work very hard.

2. **False** — Sarah says she is not working at her office today because they are singing tonight in London.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **YOUNG LOVE is a new band (made up of Sarah Jones, Pete Parsons, and Bette Lord).** — The passage asks 'Who are they?' and answers 'They're the new band YOUNG LOVE.'

2. **They are in London to play/sing tonight at The Cave.** — The passage states 'tonight they are in London. They're playing at The Cave' and Sarah says 'we're singing tonight in London.'

3. **The passage does not say whether they sing in Manchester; it only says they work in Manchester from Monday to Friday and sing in pubs and discos at the weekend (and tonight in London).** — The text distinguishes between their weekday jobs in Manchester and their weekend singing in pubs and discos, without specifying whether those pubs and discos are in Manchester or elsewhere.

4. **She usually works in an office in Manchester (from 9.00 to 5.00).** — The passage says Sarah works in Manchester as a secretary, and Sarah says 'My office opens at 9.00 and I usually work from 9.00 to 5.00.'

5. **Because the band is singing tonight in London.** — Sarah says, 'I'm not working today because we're singing tonight in London.'


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **C) Balancing a daytime job and a music band makes her very busy** — The band members work in Manchester Monday to Friday and perform at weekends, and Sarah says 'It isn't easy. I'm very busy' because of having two jobs.

2. **A) Performing music** — In 'They're playing at The Cave', 'playing' means performing music as a band.

3. **D) Three people with weekday jobs who also perform in a band** — The passage explains that Young Love has three members: Sarah (secretary), Pete (taxi driver), and Bette (music teacher), all working two jobs.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (About the Band Young Love):**

Young Love is a new band with three members from Manchester: Sarah is a secretary, Pete is a taxi driver, and Bette is a music teacher. From Monday to Friday they work at their regular jobs, and at the weekend they sing in pubs and discos. Tonight they are performing at The Cave on Lower Harrington Street in London at 8:30, and tickets cost £4.


**Task 2 (Having Two Jobs):**

Having two jobs is very difficult, just like Sarah Jones says, because people do not get enough time to rest. Working from nine to five every weekday and then singing at weekends leaves little time for family or sleep. However, if the second job is something you truly love, like playing music in a band, it can also bring a lot of joy and extra money. For me, one good job is usually better for a healthy life.


---


## Passage 17: Various Advertisements (`raw1_017` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **12 / twelve**

2. **Bristol**

3. **classical music / classical**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **C) At the University of Texas Social Club** — The advertisement for The Big Boys concert states that it takes place on Friday and Saturday at 8:00 p.m. at the University of Texas Social Club.

2. **B) General English, Specialist English, and Computer Courses** — Edwards College of English offers General English, Specialist English (Business, Import/Export, Hotel Operations), and Computer Courses.

3. **A) 7 dollars 50** — The Berkeley Quartet is playing at the Concert Hall at 8:30 p.m., and admission is 7 dollars 50.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — West End School of English offers an accommodation service as well as special classes for the Cambridge First Certificate.

2. **False** — The Foreign Friends Agency helps people find a pen-friend to practise French, German, Spanish, or Italian.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **True** — West End School of English is in central London (21 Old Street, London W2 1QB), and its telephone number is 01-402 7235.

2. **False** — According to the opera advertisement, a ticket for Madam Butterfly costs £18.50, not £8.00.

3. **True** — The Big Boys rock concert is at 8:00 p.m. on Friday and Saturday.

4. **False** — Richard James is listed as the Director of Edwards College of English and Further Studies, not the school secretary.

5. **False** — The Foreign Friends Agency is located in Bristol (23 Ramford Rd., Bristol, BS3 7HZ), not in Brighton.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **C) Language courses, a pen-friend service, and musical events** — The texts include advertisements for two language schools, a pen-friend agency, a rock concert, an opera production, and a classical quartet concert.

2. **B) Edwards College of English and the Foreign Friends Agency** — Both Edwards College of English (Bristol, England) and the Foreign Friends Agency (Bristol, BS3 7HZ) are located in Bristol.

3. **D) Stages again** — In the opera advertisement, 'revives' means that the English National Opera is staging a new production of an older opera, Madam Butterfly.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Comparing Two Language Schools):**

If you want to learn English in England, you have two choices. West End School of English is in central London and offers small classes of up to 12 students, a friendly atmosphere, and an accommodation service. Edwards College of English is in Bristol and offers General English, Specialist English for business or hotels, and Computer Courses.


**Task 2 (Choosing an Event or Service):**

If I could choose one event from the advertisements, I would go to the Berkeley Quartet concert. First, I enjoy classical music because it is relaxing and inspiring. Second, a newspaper writer from the Evening Star says they are a great group, so I know the performance will be very good. Finally, the ticket price is only seven dollars and fifty cents, which is quite cheap for a live concert at 8:30 p.m.

## Passage 18: San Francisco (`raw1_018` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **California**

2. **symbol**

3. **prison**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **B) People from Spain** — The passage states that first, people from Spain lived in San Francisco, and today Austrians, Italians, Chinese, and others live there with Americans.

2. **C) Ships passing under and cars crossing the Golden Gate Bridge** — At Fisherman's Wharf, visitors can eat seafood in a restaurant near the sea and look at the Golden Gate Bridge, watching ships and cars.

3. **D) Because of its sunny weather and lively nightlife** — The passage explains that San Francisco feels more European, the sun always shines there, and there are many nightlife options such as nightclubs, discos, restaurants, cinemas, and theatres.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — The passage clearly states that people think San Francisco is the capital of California, but it is not.

2. **True** — According to the passage, the houses, post boxes, and telephones in Chinatown make that part of the town look just like China.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **It is in the State of California in the west of the United States, near the Pacific Ocean.** — The first two sentences state that San Francisco is in the State of California in the west of the United States, near the Pacific Ocean.

2. **No, it isn't.** — The passage states: "People think it is the capital of California, but it isn't."

3. **People from Spain came to San Francisco first.** — The passage says: "First, people from Spain lived there."

4. **Chinatown, Fisherman's Wharf, and Alcatraz (and the Golden Gate Bridge).** — Tourists visit Chinatown, Fisherman's Wharf (to see the Golden Gate Bridge), and take a boat trip to Alcatraz.

5. **The Chinese live in Chinatown.** — The second paragraph begins with: "The Chinese live in Chinatown."

6. **You can watch the ships passing under the Golden Gate Bridge and the cars crossing it.** — At Fisherman's Wharf, you can look at the Golden Gate Bridge and watch the ships passing under it and the cars crossing it.

7. **The Golden Gate Bridge is the symbol of San Francisco.** — The third paragraph states: "The Golden Gate Bridge is the symbol of San Francisco."

8. **Alcatraz was a famous prison (between 1866 and 1920).** — The fourth paragraph says: "Alcatraz was a famous prison between 1866 and 1920".

9. **No, there aren't. Only tourists go there now.** — The passage states: "only tourists go there now."

10. **It is more European, the sun always shines there, and there are a lot of things you can do at night.** — The final paragraph explains that San Francisco is more European, sunny, and offers many nightclubs, discos, restaurants, cinemas, and theatres.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **A) To introduce San Francisco's culture, attractions, and atmosphere** — The passage describes San Francisco's location, diverse residents, main tourist attractions (Chinatown, Fisherman's Wharf, Golden Gate Bridge, Alcatraz), and lifestyle.

2. **C) San Francisco is home to people from many different cultures** — Because people from Spain, Austria, Italy, China, and America live there, and Chinatown looks just like China, the reader can infer that San Francisco is a very multicultural city.

3. **B) The Golden Gate Bridge** — In the sentence "After you finish your meal, you can take a boat trip to Alcatraz," "it" is not used; in "You can watch the ships passing under it and the cars crossing it," "it" refers to the Golden Gate Bridge mentioned in the previous sentence.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Describing San Francisco):**

San Francisco is a sunny city in California near the Pacific Ocean. People from many countries live there, and Chinatown looks just like China. Tourists enjoy eating seafood at Fisherman's Wharf while watching ships and cars at the Golden Gate Bridge. They also take boat trips to Alcatraz, an old prison. Because it feels European and has great nightlife, many young people want to live there.


**Task 2 (A Trip to San Francisco):**

I would love to visit San Francisco because it has many exciting places to see. First, I really want to eat fresh seafood at Fisherman's Wharf and look at the famous Golden Gate Bridge. I also want to walk around Chinatown because the streets, houses, and restaurants sound very interesting. In addition, taking a boat trip to the old prison of Alcatraz would be an unforgettable experience. Finally, the sunny weather and lively nightlife make it a wonderful city for a holiday.


---


## Passage 19: Letter from Halima and Ahmed (`raw1_019` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **fifteen million / 15 million**

2. **1792**

3. **dogs**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **A) With their American friend, Tom** — The passage introduces Halima and Ahmed as sister and brother who left their home in Egypt last week and are visiting Washington D.C. with their American friend, Tom.

2. **C) The Lincoln Memorial, the Vietnam Veterans Memorial, and the Capitol** — According to the letter, they took a tour on Friday and saw the Lincoln Memorial, the Vietnam Veterans Memorial, and the Capitol.

3. **D) Because they arrived after it closed at 7 p.m.** — They wanted to visit the National Air and Space Museum on Saturday, but it closes at 7 p.m. and they arrived at 7:30 p.m., so they hope to see it tomorrow.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — In the letter, Halima and Ahmed write that John Adams was the first president to live in the White House.

2. **Not Given** — The passage mentions that they plan to see the National Air and Space Museum tomorrow, but it does not say how long they will stay in the United States.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **Fifteen million people visit Washington D.C. every year.** — The second sentence states: "Fifteen million people visit the city every year."

2. **They are from Egypt.** — The passage says that Halima and Ahmed "left their home in Egypt and travelled to the United States last week."

3. **Her name is Samira (Aunt Samira).** — They address their letter to "Dear Aunt Samira,".

4. **Yes, they do.** — In their letter, they write: "What a great city!"

5. **They visited the Capitol on Friday.** — The letter says: "On Friday we took a tour. We saw many historical sites: the Lincoln Memorial, the Vietnam Veterans Memorial, and the Capitol."

6. **They are historical sites.** — The letter says: "We saw many historical sites: the Lincoln Memorial, the Vietnam Veterans Memorial, and the Capitol."

7. **No, they aren't. They are staying at a hotel.** — In the letter, they write: "We left our hotel and went to the White House."

8. **It's a big, white building.** — The letter describes the White House as: "It's a big, white building."

9. **They took a picture of two dogs chasing each other in the White House garden.** — They write: "we saw two dogs in the garden. They were chasing each other. We took a picture of them."

10. **No, they didn't. It closes at 7 p.m. and they arrived at 7:30 p.m.** — The letter explains: "We wanted to see the National Air and Space Museum too, but it closes at 7 p.m. and we went there at 7:30 p.m."


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **B) Excited and enthusiastic about their trip** — Halima and Ahmed write excitedly ("What a great city!") about their tours, historical sites, the White House, and museums, and say they hope to see another museum tomorrow.

2. **C) They visited historical sites on Friday and museums on Saturday** — The letter says: "On Friday we took a tour... On Saturday, we were busy, too... We hope to see it tomorrow." Since Saturday was yesterday or today (the day they went to the White House), and they say "We arrived in Washington, D.C. three days ago" and "hope to see it tomorrow," they are writing on Saturday evening (or Sunday) after visiting the museums. Wait: they write "On Saturday, we were busy, too," using the past tense, which means Saturday has finished or it is Saturday night/Sunday, and they have already spent at least Friday and Saturday sightseeing.

3. **A) The National Air and Space Museum** — In "We wanted to see the National Air and Space Museum too, but it closes at 7 p.m. and we went there at 7:30 p.m. We hope to see it tomorrow," "it" refers to the National Air and Space Museum.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (A Summary of Their Trip):**

Halima and Ahmed are visiting Washington D.C. from Egypt with their friend Tom. On Friday, they took a tour and saw famous historical sites, including the Lincoln Memorial and the Capitol. On Saturday, they visited the White House, took a photo of two dogs in the garden, and went to some museums. However, they arrived too late to enter the National Air and Space Museum.


**Task 2 (A Holiday Letter Home):**

Dear Deniz,

I arrived in Istanbul two days ago, and I am having a wonderful time! Yesterday I took a city tour and visited several historical sites. First, I went to Topkapi Palace and learned about its history. In the afternoon, I walked around the Grand Bazaar and bought some souvenirs. Tomorrow I want to visit the Hagia Sophia early in the morning before it gets crowded. I took lots of photos to show you!

Best wishes,
Ali

## Passage 20: Derek (`raw1_020` · `A1`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **jockey**

2. **55 / fifty-five**

3. **three / 3**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **B) He wasn't a good student at school** — The passage states that Derek Matthews wasn't a good student at school.

2. **C) In Ascot, near London** — The passage explains that his riding school is in Ascot, a few miles from London.

3. **A) He visits his horses and rides them** — The passage states that he drives to Ascot three days a week, visits his horses, rides them, and enjoys his days at the riding school a lot.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — The passage says that today, at 55, Derek is very famous and has got a riding school in Ascot.

2. **Not Given** — The passage mentions that he wanted to be a jockey when he was younger, and now he has a riding school at 55, but it never states whether he actually worked as a professional jockey in the past.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **Because he liked horses.** — The second sentence states: "He liked horses so he wanted to be a jockey."

2. **He enjoys his days at the riding school a lot.** — The last sentence says: "He says he enjoys his days at the riding school a lot."

3. **No, he wasn't.** — The first sentence says: "Derek Matthews wasn't a good student at school."

4. **He drives to Ascot (by car).** — The passage says: "He drives to Ascot three days a week".

5. **It is a few miles from London.** — The passage describes Ascot as "a few miles from London."


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **C) Derek turned his childhood love for horses into a happy career** — The passage contrasts Derek's past (not a good student, loved horses) with his present success (at 55, famous owner of a riding school in Ascot who enjoys his work).

2. **D) He probably lives somewhere outside the riding school and travels there** — Since Derek "drives to Ascot three days a week" and Ascot is "a few miles from London," we can infer that he probably does not live at the riding school in Ascot.

3. **B) His horses** — In the sentence "He drives to Ascot three days a week, visits his horses and rides them," "them" refers back to "his horses."


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (About Derek Matthews):**

Derek Matthews was not a good student, and he wanted to be a jockey because he loved horses. Now he is 55 years old and very famous. He owns a riding school in Ascot, near London. He drives there three times a week to see and ride his horses, and he is very happy with his life.


**Task 2 (School and Happiness at Work):**

In my opinion, you can be successful even if you are not a top student at school. Derek Matthews was not a good student, but he loved horses and worked with his passion. Today he is famous and enjoys his riding school very much. Everyone has different talents. Some people are good at maths or science, while others are good at sports, music, or art. When you do something you love, you can become happy and successful.

## Passage 21: Roland, Evi, and Della (`raw1_021` · `A1`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **eleven / 11**

2. **guitar**

3. **restaurant**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **B) In New York** — The passage states that Roland's mother and father live in New York.

2. **C) An English teacher** — Evi studies English at university and wants to be an English teacher.

3. **D) At a cafe** — Evi says that her band plays in the evenings at a cafe.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — The passage explicitly says that Roland goes skiing in the Alps every winter.

2. **False** — The passage states that Evi isn't married, although she has an Italian boyfriend called Luigi.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **Pop music.** — Evi says many people like pop music and come to the cafe to listen to New Band.

2. **He plays basketball and goes skiing in the Alps every winter.** — The passage mentions that Roland is a good basketball player and goes skiing in the Alps every winter.

3. **In New York.** — Della is a student at a school for foreign students in New York.

Roland Kohl is an engineer from Berlin who loves basketball and skiing. Evi Pappas lives near Athens. She studies English, plays the guitar in a band, and speaks four languages. Della Chan is from Hong Kong, and she is studying English in New York because she wants to talk with customers in her restaurant.


**Task 2 (My Life and Plans):**

I live in Izmir, and I am a university student. I am an active person, so I enjoy playing football and swimming in my free time. I speak Turkish and a little English, and I study English every day because I want to travel to different countries. In the future, I want to work as a software engineer in a big city.

## Passage 22: Letter from Pam (`raw1_022` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **USA / U.S.A.**

2. **twelve / 12**

3. **father**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **C) From Tokyo** — The introduction states that Pam wrote the letter to her father from Tokyo.

2. **B) She didn't have any time.** — Pam writes that she wanted to phone her father from Istanbul, but she didn't have any time.

3. **C) Not very good, but cheap** — Pam says the lunch at the restaurant near the airport wasn't very good, but it was cheap.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — Pam writes that she was in England, Canada, and Turkey last month.

2. **False** — Pam tells her father to wait for her next letter from Berlin because they are going there next.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **About two months ago (this is her second month in the job).** — Pam writes to her father that this is her second month in this job.

2. **Yes, she does.** — She writes that she likes it very much and thinks she has got a wonderful job.

3. **Three countries (England, Canada, and Turkey).** — She writes that she was in England, Canada, and Turkey last month.

4. **No, she didn't.** — She wanted to phone her father from Istanbul, but she didn't have any time.

5. **She was in Istanbul.** — She writes, "Yesterday we were in Istanbul again."

6. **Yes, they were.** — They were free for twelve hours in Istanbul and went out for lunch.

7. **No, they didn't.** — They went to a restaurant near the airport and had lunch there.

8. **It was near the airport.** — She says they went to a restaurant near the airport.

9. **No, it wasn't very good, but it was cheap.** — She writes that the meal at the restaurant near the airport wasn't very good, though it was cheap.

10. **They saw a lot of interesting places, the people, the houses, and the food.** — When they went to the city, they saw a lot of interesting places, the people, the houses, and the food.

11. **They were free for twelve hours.** — She states that they were free for twelve hours in Istanbul.

12. **Yes, she does.** — She writes that everything is wonderful in Tokyo, too.

13. **She wants to buy some things for her father.** — She writes, "I want to buy some things for you here."

14. **They are going to Berlin.** — She tells her father to wait for her next letter from Berlin because they are going there.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **B) A flight attendant on airplanes** — Pam works for a big airline company and flies to many countries, which describes a flight attendant (stewardess).

2. **C) Because she had free time and wanted to eat and see the city** — They had twelve hours of free time and didn't want to stay at the airport, so they ate lunch nearby and then explored the city.

3. **A) She enjoys it very much, even though it can be tiring.** — Pam says she likes her job very much and thinks it is wonderful, even though she was very tired last month.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Summary of Pam's Travels):**

Pam Robinson is an American stewardess who started her job two months ago. Last month, she visited England, Canada, and Turkey. Yesterday, she had twelve free hours in Istanbul. She ate lunch near the airport and explored the city. Now she is in Tokyo, where she wants to buy gifts for her father before flying to Berlin next.


**Task 2 (Working and Traveling):**

Jobs with a lot of travel, like working as a stewardess, have both good and bad sides. First, traveling lets you see interesting cities, meet new people, and try different foods around the world. Every week feels exciting and new. However, flying to many countries can also make you very tired, and you sometimes do not have enough time to call or visit your family. In my opinion, a travel job is wonderful for young people who love adventure.

## Passage 23: Joan (`raw1_023` · `A1`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **interesting**

2. **two / 2**

3. **pictures**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **B) A very good student** — The passage begins by saying Joan was a very good student when she was at school.

2. **C) In an office** — The passage states that now she enjoys the work in the office very much.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — The passage says she finished school two years ago and started to work as a computer programmer.

2. **False** — The passage explicitly states that she enjoys the work in the office very much.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **Because computers were very interesting for her.** — The passage says computers were very interesting for her, so she wanted to work with computers.

2. **She enjoys the work in the office very much.** — The passage states, "Now she enjoys the work in the office very much."

3. **Two years ago.** — She finished school two years ago and started to work as a computer programmer.

4. **She can draw pictures with computers.** — The last sentence states that she can draw pictures with computers.

5. **Question: Was Joan a good student at school? Answer: Yes, she was a very good student.** — The first sentence states that Joan was a very good student when she was at school.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

2. **B) She began working as a computer programmer.** — She finished school two years ago and started to work as a computer programmer.

3. **C) She likes it very much.** — Saying she enjoys the work in the office very much shows that she likes her job a lot.


### Writing Model Cevapları

Joan was a very good student at school. She liked computers very much and wanted a job with them. Two years ago, she finished school and became a computer programmer. Now she works in an office, enjoys her job, and can draw pictures on computers.


**Task 2 (My Interests and Future Job):**

When I was at school, English and science were very interesting for me. I always liked learning new things, so I studied hard. Now I want to be a doctor or a teacher in the future. I think working with people is exciting. I can also use a computer well, and this skill helps me with my lessons every day.

## Passage 24: Mahatma Gandhi (`raw1_024` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **1869**

2. **lawyer**

3. **poor**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **A) Thirteen** — The passage states that Gandhi got married at the age of thirteen.

2. **C) Twenty years later** — After going to work in South Africa, he returned to India twenty years later.

3. **C) In 1948** — The final sentence says a fanatic killed him in 1948.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — The passage says life was difficult when he was young.

2. **True** — The passage explicitly states that he played a very important role in the independence of India.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **In India.** — The first sentence states that Mahatma Gandhi was born in India in 1869.

2. **In 1869.** — The passage states that he was born in India in 1869.

3. **Because it was a common custom in India at that time.** — The passage explains that getting married at the age of thirteen was a common custom in India at that time.

4. **To become a lawyer.** — The passage says he went to London and became a lawyer.

5. **In South Africa.** — He went to work in South Africa and organised demonstrations there.

6. **He returned to India after becoming a lawyer in London, and again twenty years after going to work in South Africa.** — The passage mentions returning to his country after London and returning to India twenty years after going to South Africa.

7. **The poor.** — The text says that in India, he worked hard to help the poor.

8. **A fanatic killed him.** — The final sentence states that a fanatic killed him in 1948.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **C) He organised demonstrations and went to prison several times.** — The text states that in South Africa, Gandhi organised demonstrations and was sent to prison several times.

2. **A) A traditional social practice** — Getting married at thirteen was a traditional practice in Indian society at that time.

3. **C) The life and leadership of Mahatma Gandhi** — The passage gives an overview of Gandhi's birth, education, work in South Africa, role in Indian independence, and death.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Life of Mahatma Gandhi):**

Mahatma Gandhi was born in India in 1869 and got married at thirteen. After studying law and becoming a lawyer in London, he worked in South Africa for twenty years, where he organised demonstrations and went to prison. Later, he returned to India, helped poor people, and played a major role in his country's independence before he was killed in 1948.


**Task 2 (What Makes a Great Leader?):**

In my opinion, a great leader should be brave, honest, and helpful. First, a leader must care about ordinary and poor people and work hard to improve their lives. Second, a good leader does not give up easily when life is difficult. For example, Gandhi went to prison many times, but he continued to fight for his country's independence. Leaders who help others and stay strong are always remembered in history.
