## Passage 25: William Shakespeare (`raw1_025` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **The Rose / Rose**

2. **actors**

3. **forty-six / 46**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **B) His father was an important man in town.** — The speaker mentions that John Shakespeare was an important man in Stratford and William did not come from a poor family.

2. **C) A fire from a stage gun burned it down.** — According to the passage, a gun was used in the play Henry VIII, and fire from the gun burned the Globe Theatre down.

3. **A) To become an actor at the best theatres** — The passage states that Shakespeare went to London in 1587 because he wanted to be an actor and the best theatres were there.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — The passage states that William married Anne Hathaway in Stratford when he was eighteen, before going to London in 1587.

2. **True** — The speaker states that Shakespeare worked for King James the First in 1603 and performed his plays for the King and his friends.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **He was eighteen years old.** — Paragraph 2 states: "When he was eighteen, William married Anne Hathaway in Stratford."

2. **Anne Hathaway** — Paragraph 2 states: "William married Anne Hathaway in Stratford."

3. **He wanted to be an actor and the best theatres were in London.** — Paragraph 2 explains: "He wanted to be an actor and the best theatres were in London. So, in 1587, William went to London..."

4. **The Rose** — Paragraph 2 says: "...in London, where he worked as an actor in a theatre called The Rose."

5. **He was famous for his plays.** — Paragraph 3 says: "Everyone liked his plays, and he became famous."

6. **He was born in Stratford.** — Paragraph 1 says: "William Shakespeare was born in Stratford on April 23rd, 1564."

7. **He married Anne Hathaway.** — Shakespeare was born in 1564 and married Anne Hathaway when he was eighteen (1564 + 18 = 1582).

8. **He went to London.** — Paragraph 2 says: "So, in 1587, William went to London, where he worked as an actor in a theatre called The Rose."

9. **He worked for King James I.** — Paragraph 4 says: "When James I became King in 1603, Shakespeare worked for him..."

10. **He went back to Stratford and died.** — Paragraph 5 says: "In 1610 Shakespeare went back to Stratford... he died on April 23rd, 1610."


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **C) Key events in William Shakespeare's life and career** — The passage mainly describes Shakespeare's birth, marriage, career as an actor and playwright in London, and return to Stratford.

2. **B) Shakespeare worked at more than one theatre during his career in London.** — The passage says Shakespeare was born in 1564, went to London in 1587, wrote many famous plays, worked for the King in 1603, and returned to Stratford in 1610. The Globe Theatre burned down during his last play, Henry VIII.

3. **D) staged** — In "This theatre presented his last play, Henry VIII", "presented" means staged or put on a performance of the play for an audience.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Guided Summary: William Shakespeare's Life):**

William Shakespeare was born in Stratford in 1564. At eighteen, he married Anne Hathaway, and in 1587 he moved to London to become an actor at The Rose theatre. Soon he started writing plays such as Romeo and Juliet and became very famous. Later, he performed for King James I and worked at the Globe Theatre. In 1610, he returned to Stratford and died at the age of forty-six.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: Moving to a Big City for Success):**

Many young people today still leave their hometowns to move to big cities for work, just like Shakespeare moved to London. Big cities offer more jobs, better training, and bigger audiences for talented people. For example, someone who wants to work in theatre, film, or technology can meet experienced people and find better opportunities in a large city. However, living far from family can be difficult and expensive. In my opinion, moving to a big city is a good idea when you are young and want to build your career, even if you plan to return home later.


---


## Passage 26: Alfred Hitchcock (`raw1_026` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **daughter**

2. **Hollywood**

3. **1980**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **C) He got married and began working for a film company.** — The passage states that Hitchcock started working with a British film company and married Alma Revelle in 1920.

2. **B) 53 films** — The speaker notes that Hitchcock lived in Los Angeles for the rest of his life and made many of his 53 films there.

3. **D) Los Angeles in 1980** — The passage states that Queen Elizabeth the Second made him 'Sir Alfred' in 1980, and soon after that, in May 1980, he died in Los Angeles.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — The passage states that Hitchcock made Psycho in 1960 and The Birds in 1963, after he had already left Britain in 1939 to live in Los Angeles.

2. **True** — The passage states that he married Alma Revelle in 1920 and she was his wife for the next sixty years.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **In London** — Paragraph 1 says: "Alfred Joseph Hitchcock was born in London on August 13, 1899. He went to school and university there..."

2. **He started to work with a British film company and he married Alma Revelle.** — Paragraph 1 states: "...in 1920 he started to work with a British film company. In 1920 he also married Alma Revelle..."

3. **He had one child (one daughter).** — Paragraph 1 states: "They had one daughter."

4. **The 39 Steps and The Man Who Knew Too Much** — Paragraph 2 says: "From 1925 to 1939 he made several films in Britain, including The 39 Steps and The Man Who Knew Too Much."

5. **In Los Angeles (Hollywood)** — Paragraph 3 states: "He lived in Los Angeles for the rest of his life and made many of his 53 films there."

6. **Psycho (1960) and The Birds (1963)** — Paragraph 3 says: "A lot of people in America and Europe saw his famous films Psycho (1960) and The Birds (1963)."

7. **Queen Elizabeth II** — Paragraph 4 states: "Queen Elizabeth II made Hitchcock 'Sir Alfred' in 1980."


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **A) He spent a longer part of his career working in the USA than in Britain.** — The passage states that he left Britain in 1939 to work in Hollywood, lived in Los Angeles for the rest of his life, and made many of his 53 films there.

2. **C) London** — In "He went to school and university there", "there" refers back to London, which was mentioned in the previous sentence ("born in London").

3. **B) until he died in 1980** — "For the rest of his life" means from 1939 until his death in 1980.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Guided Summary: The Career of Alfred Hitchcock):**

Alfred Hitchcock was born in London in 1899 and studied there. In 1920, he joined a British film company and married Alma Revelle. Between 1925 and 1939, he directed several films in Britain before moving to Hollywood. He lived in Los Angeles for the rest of his life and made famous films like Psycho and The Birds. In 1980, Queen Elizabeth II named him 'Sir Alfred', and he died later that year.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: Your Favourite Type of Film):**

I really enjoy watching mystery and thriller films, especially at the weekend. My favourite film is Inception because the story is very clever and keeps the audience guessing until the very end. Like Hitchcock's films, a good thriller makes you feel excited and curious about what will happen next. I prefer watching suspenseful films rather than simple comedies because they make me think. In my opinion, strong acting and mysterious music are the most important parts of a memorable film.


---


## Passage 27: Charlie Chaplin (`raw1_027` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **music halls**

2. **1914**

3. **Switzerland**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **C) He started working at the Keystone Company in Hollywood.** — The passage says he went to the USA in 1910 with Fred Karno's company, didn't stay very long, and in 1913 started working at the Keystone Company in Hollywood.

2. **B) the little tramp** — According to the passage, Chaplin introduced the little tramp in his second film, Kid Auto Races at Venice (1914), and this character made him famous.

3. **A) 1943** — The passage states that Chaplin met Oona O'Neill in 1942 and married her a year later, in 1943, when he was 54 and she was 18.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — The passage says he stayed with Fred Karno's company for a short time ("didn't stay with Karno very long") after going to the USA in 1910.

2. **True** — The speaker explicitly states that Chaplin left the USA in 1952 for political reasons.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **In London** — Paragraph 1 states: "Charlie Chaplin was born in London in 1889."

2. **In 1889** — Paragraph 1 states: "Charlie Chaplin was born in London in 1889."

3. **In music halls** — Paragraph 1 says: "He first appeared in music halls and then acted on the London stage."

4. **To the USA** — Paragraph 1 states: "In 1910 he went to the USA with Fred Karno's company..."

5. **He started to work at the Keystone Company in Hollywood.** — Paragraph 1 says: "In 1913 he started to work at the Keystone Company in Hollywood."

6. **In 1914** — Paragraph 1 states: "There he made his first film, Making A Living, in 1914."

7. **The little tramp** — Paragraph 1 says: "In his second film, Kid Auto Races at Venice (1914), he introduced the little tramp."


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **B) Chaplin had stage acting experience before he began making films.** — Paragraph 1 shows that Chaplin performed in music halls and on the London stage before moving to the USA and later starting his film career in Hollywood.

2. **D) presented for the first time** — In "In his second film, Kid Auto Races at Venice (1914), he introduced the little tramp", "introduced" means showed or presented a new character to the public for the first time.

3. **A) Chaplin spent the last twenty-five years of his life in Switzerland.** — Chaplin married Oona O'Neill in 1943 (a year after meeting her in 1942), left the USA in 1952, and lived in Switzerland until his death in 1977, meaning he spent about 25 years in Switzerland.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Guided Summary: Charlie Chaplin's Life):**

Charlie Chaplin was born in London in 1889. After performing in music halls and theatres, he moved to the USA in 1910 and joined Hollywood's Keystone Company in 1913. In 1914, he made his first film and then became famous as the little tramp in his second film. Chaplin married several times, including Oona O'Neill in 1943. In 1952, he left the USA for political reasons and lived in Switzerland until his death in 1977.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: Are Silent Comedies Still Funny?):**

Silent films like Charlie Chaplin's can still make people laugh today because physical comedy is easy for everyone to understand. Even without spoken words, facial expressions and body movements tell a clear story. Anyone from any country can watch the little tramp and understand his feelings immediately. Modern films often use fast dialogue and special effects, but simple humour never gets old. Therefore, I believe classic silent comedies will always stay entertaining for new generations.


---


## Passage 28: Super Tanker Disaster (`raw1_028` · `A1`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **English Channel**

2. **cargo ship**

3. **stormy**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **B) At about two in the morning** — The report says the accident happened at about two this morning.

2. **C) An English oil tanker and an Italian cargo ship** — The report states that an English oil tanker crashed into an Italian cargo ship.

3. **A) Because of very stormy weather** — According to the report, people say the accident happened because it was very stormy.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — The report states that it was an English oil tanker and an Italian cargo ship, not a French tanker.

2. **Not Given** — The news snippet does not mention whether anyone was hurt or how many people were on the ships.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **At about two this morning.** — The report states: "There was a bad accident in the English Channel at about two this morning."

2. **It was very stormy.** — The report states: "They say that the accident happened because it was very stormy."


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **B) A short newspaper report** — The text starts with "Daily News" and ends with "... (more on page 8)", which is the format of a short front-page newspaper report.

2. **D) Two ships from different countries were involved in the crash.** — The report mentions "an English oil tanker" and "an Italian cargo ship" crashing into each other in the English Channel.

3. **C) windy and rough with bad weather** — The text says "...because it was very stormy", which describes bad weather with strong winds and rain at sea.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Guided Short Response: News Summary):**

Early this morning at about two o'clock, there was a serious accident at sea in the English Channel. An English oil tanker hit an Italian cargo ship. The weather was very stormy at that time, and people think the bad weather caused the crash.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: Travel Safety in Bad Weather):**

Bad weather often makes travel dangerous on roads and at sea. When there is a strong storm, heavy rain, or thick fog, drivers and ship captains cannot see clearly. Strong winds and high waves can also push ships off their path and cause accidents. To stay safe in bad weather, people should check the weather forecast before they travel. Sometimes it is much safer to wait until the storm stops.


---


## Passage 29: Selton Times (`raw1_029` · `A1`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **four-floor / four floor**

2. **8 p.m. / 8 pm / eight p.m.**

3. **closed**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **B) It has stores for clothes and shoes, plus cafes and restaurants.** — The announcement mentions 'everything from books to shoes' and 'fashionable clothes', as well as breakfast, lunch, and dinner at cafes and restaurants.

2. **C) 7 am to 10 pm** — On Saturday, both cafes/restaurants and stores are open from 7 am to 10 pm.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — The announcement is dated Wednesday, February 16th and states that the centre 'opened yesterday' (Tuesday, February 15th).

2. **True** — From Monday to Friday, cafes and restaurants stay open until 9 pm, whereas stores close earlier at 8 pm.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **Books and shoes (or fashionable clothes).** — The passage states that the building has everything from books to shoes, and you can find fashionable clothes for your family and friends.

2. **No, there are at least two shopping centres in Selton.** — The headline announces 'SELTON'S SECOND SHOPPING CENTRE OPENED YESTERDAY!', meaning it is the second one in town.

3. **No, you cannot.** — The schedule clearly states 'Centre closed on Sundays.'

4. **Yes, they are.** — On Saturdays, the stores are open from 7 am to 10 pm, so they are open at 9 am.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **C) To announce a new shopping centre and share its opening hours** — The article introduces Selton's newest shopping centre, describes what visitors can buy and eat there, and provides its weekly opening hours.

2. **B) Cafes stay open one hour later than stores on weekdays.** — On weekdays, stores close at 8 pm, while cafes and restaurants close at 9 pm—one hour later. On Saturday, both close at 10 pm.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Guided Summary: New Shopping Centre):**

Selton's second shopping centre opened on Tuesday in a four-floor building. Visitors can buy books, shoes, and fashionable clothes there, and they can eat breakfast, lunch, or dinner at the cafes and restaurants. From Monday to Friday, cafes and restaurants are open from 7 am to 9 pm, while stores are open from 7 am to 8 pm. On Saturdays, everything is open from 7 am to 10 pm, and the centre is closed on Sundays.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: Shopping Centres):**

I like shopping at large shopping centres because everything is in one place. When I need clothes, shoes, or books, I do not have to walk to different streets in cold or rainy weather. I also enjoy meeting my friends at a cafe for lunch after shopping. However, I think shopping centres should close on Sundays so that workers can rest and spend time with their families. Overall, a modern shopping centre is very comfortable and useful for families.

## Passage 30: Lebrat's Visit (`raw1_030` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **French president / president**

2. **1991**

3. **Madrid**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **B) To meet the manager of the local MAGROS store** — The passage states he is in Selton to meet Mr. Douglas, the manager of the MAGROS store in Oxham Street.

2. **C) England** — There are 20 in England, 12 in Germany, 15 in France, and 18 in Spain, making England the country with the most stores.

3. **A) Rome, Italy** — The passage says 'Next year, Mr. Lebrat wants to open a store in Rome, Italy, too.'


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — This is his second visit to Selton; his first visit was in 1991 when he opened the store.

2. **True** — The passage notes that he cannot stay in Selton for very long because he has a busy programme and is flying to Madrid tomorrow morning.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **False** — The passage introduces him as 'the French president of the MAGROS Food company,' so he is from France, not Spain.

2. **False** — He arrived 'yesterday.' Since this article follows the Wednesday, February 16th edition of the Selton Times, yesterday was Tuesday, not Thursday.

3. **False** — He is in Selton to meet Mr. Douglas, the manager of the existing MAGROS store in Oxham Street, not to open a new store.

4. **True** — The passage states that he was in Selton in 1991 when he opened the store.

5. **False** — The text names only one manager, Mr. Douglas ('the manager of the MAGROS store in Oxham Street').

6. **False** — He is flying to Madrid 'tomorrow morning.' In the Wednesday edition of the Selton Times, tomorrow morning is Thursday morning, not Wednesday morning.

7. **True** — The passage says he is flying to Madrid tomorrow morning 'to visit the stores there,' showing there are MAGROS stores in Madrid.

8. **20** — The text states there are 'twenty in England.'

9. **12** — The text states there are 'twelve in Germany.'

10. **15** — The text states there are 'fifteen in France.'

11. **18** — The text states there are 'eighteen in Spain.'

12. **0** — There are no stores in Italy yet; Mr. Lebrat wants to open one in Rome next year (20 + 12 + 15 + 18 = 65).


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **B) He plans to expand the company into Italy next year.** — There are currently 65 stores across four countries, and Mr. Lebrat plans to add a store in Rome, Italy next year, bringing the total into a fifth country.

2. **D) a planned schedule** — In the context of 'He has a busy programme. He is flying to Madrid tomorrow morning', 'programme' means a planned schedule of events and travel.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Summary: Mr. Lebrat's Visit):**

Christian Lebrat, the French president of the MAGROS Food company, is currently visiting Selton to meet Mr. Douglas, the manager of the local store in Oxham Street. This is his second visit since he opened the store in 1991. Currently, MAGROS operates sixty-five stores across England, Germany, France, and Spain, and Mr. Lebrat hopes to open a new store in Rome next year. Because of his busy schedule, he is flying to Madrid tomorrow morning.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: International Stores):**

I think international food companies can bring both advantages and disadvantages to a town. On the positive side, a large store offers many different products, good prices, and new jobs for local people. For example, families can find fresh food easily in one big shop. On the other hand, big companies can make life difficult for small neighbourhood markets that cannot compete with low prices. In my opinion, international stores are helpful as long as people also continue to support small local shops.


---


## Passage 31: The Buzz Survey (`raw1_031` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **weekly**

2. **detective stories**

3. **doctor's office / doctors office**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **C) School books** — Jenny says she does not buy any books and only reads her school books.

2. **D) He thinks it has no real news in it.** — Eric says he never reads The Sun because there isn't any news in it.

3. **B) Eric** — Eric buys The Times or The Guardian sometimes and reads the newspaper almost every day, showing he prefers reading newspapers.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — Paul states, 'I like reading detective stories. They are very interesting. I hate love stories.'

2. **False** — Tom says, 'I don't buy magazines, but I always read them when I'm at the doctor's office.'


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **False** — The passage clearly describes The Buzz as 'a weekly magazine in England,' not a newspaper.

2. **False** — Paul says, 'I hate love stories,' which means he strongly dislikes them rather than not minding them.

3. **False** — Jenny says, 'I don't like reading books at all. I only read my school books.'

4. **True** — Eric says, 'My father buys The Daily Mirror,' indicating his father reads that newspaper.

5. **False** — Eric says, 'I never read The Sun because there isn't any news in it,' showing he does not think it is a good newspaper.

6. **False** — Tom says he does not buy magazines and reads them at the doctor's office, not in his own office.

7. **True** — Tom says, 'I always read them when I'm at the doctor's office,' which means there are magazines available there.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **A) To share the results of a survey on young people's reading habits** — The passage reports the answers of four students—Paul, Jenny, Eric, and Tom—to a survey about their reading preferences across books, newspapers, and magazines.

2. **C) He only reads magazines while waiting at the doctor's office.** — Tom says he never buys magazines and only reads them while waiting at the doctor's office, meaning he reads them only occasionally rather than regularly buying them.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Summary: Student Reading Habits):**

The Buzz, a weekly British magazine, asked four students about their reading habits. Paul enjoys detective stories but hates love stories. Jenny dislikes reading for pleasure and only reads her school books. Eric reads newspapers almost daily, preferring The Times or The Guardian, while his father buys The Daily Mirror; however, Eric never reads The Sun. Finally, Tom never buys magazines and only reads them at the doctor's office.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: My Reading Habits):**

I enjoy reading in my free time, especially short stories and science articles. Unlike Jenny in the survey, I think reading outside school is very relaxing and helps me learn new words. I usually do not buy printed newspapers, because I prefer reading news online on my phone. However, I still like holding a real book before going to sleep. Reading every day keeps my mind active and gives me new ideas to talk about with my friends.

## Passage 32: D.H. Lawrence (`raw1_032` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **1885**

2. **Munich**

3. **1915**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **B) Five children** — The passage states he had two brothers and two sisters, making five children in total including Lawrence himself.

2. **C) 1914** — He was born in 1885 and his mother died in 1910, when he was 25. Four years later, in 1914, he married Frieda Van Richthofen.

3. **A) Women in Love** — The passage lists Sons and Lovers (1913), The Rainbow (1915), and Women in Love (1920) as some of his famous books.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — They lived in England for a short time after getting married, and then moved to Germany and stayed near Munich for two years.

2. **Not Given** — The passage mentions his brothers, sisters, mother, and wife, but does not state whether he and Frieda had any children.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **False** — The very first sentence states that 'D.H. Lawrence was an English writer. He was born in 1885, in England.'

2. **True** — The passage says, 'He lived in Nottingham and went to school there.'

3. **False** — The text states that 'Lawrence married Frieda Van Richthofen in 1914,' so Frieda was his wife, not his mother.

4. **No Information** — The passage mentions that his mother died in 1910, but gives no information about his father.

5. **True** — The passage lists 'Sons and Lovers (1913)' as one of his famous books.

6. **No Information** — The passage says 'He died in 1930,' but does not state the country or city where he died.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **C) About 45 years old** — He was born in 1885 and died in 1930, which means he lived for 45 years (or 44 depending on month, 1930 - 1885 = 45).

2. **B) He wrote Sons and Lovers before he married Frieda.** — Sons and Lovers was written in 1913, while he married Frieda Van Richthofen in 1914, meaning he published that novel one year before his marriage.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Biography Summary: D.H. Lawrence):**

D.H. Lawrence was an English author born in 1885. He grew up in Nottingham with two brothers and two sisters, and he lost his mother in 1910 at the age of twenty-five. In 1914, he married Frieda Van Richthofen, and the couple later lived near Munich, Germany, for two years. Lawrence wrote many famous novels, including Sons and Lovers, The Rainbow, and Women in Love, before dying in 1930.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: Travel and Writing):**

I think living in another country can help a writer create much richer stories. When writers move to a new place, they meet different people, hear new languages, and see unfamiliar landscapes. These experiences give them fresh ideas and help them understand human feelings more deeply. For example, living in Germany for two years probably gave D.H. Lawrence a new view of life outside England. Even though moving away from home can be difficult, new environments often inspire great books.


---


## Passage 33: The Funniest Film (`raw1_033` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **two / 2**

2. **stalls**

3. **hated**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **C) The dog watched the film closely and cried when the hero died** — The usherette was even more surprised when the dog watched the film and was obviously interested in it, and then began to cry when the hero died.

2. **B) After the film finished** — The usherette spoke to the man after the film and said she had never seen a dog cry in a cinema before.

3. **D) The dog had hated the book the film was based on** — The man nodded and explained that he could not understand why the dog liked the film so much because the dog hated the book.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — Their seats were in the stalls, at the side, not at the front or center.

2. **True** — The passage states that when the hero died tragically, the dog began to cry like a baby.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **c) the dog liked watching films** — The man bought two tickets and the dog watched the film with obvious interest, showing that the dog was brought along as a moviegoer who liked watching films.

2. **c) the man and his dog went inside** — The passage states: "He bought two tickets and they went inside. The usherette was very surprised, but she showed them to their places."

3. **c) the dog cried when the hero died** — After the film, the usherette approached the man and said she had never seen a dog cry in a cinema before.

4. **a) The dog liked the film but hated the book.** — The dog watched the film with great interest and cried at the sad ending, yet the man remarked that the dog "hated the book."

5. **the man and his dog** — "He" (the man) took his dog to the cinema, bought two tickets, and "they" (the man and his dog) went inside.

6. **the usherette** — "The usherette was very surprised, but she showed them to their places." Here "she" refers to the usherette.

7. **the dog** — In "when the film began and the dog watched it. He was obviously interested in it.", "He" refers to the dog.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **C) To tell a humorous story with an unexpected punchline** — The story ends with a humorous twist: the man treats his dog like a regular reader and says he cannot understand why the dog enjoyed the film after hating the book.

2. **B) He responds calmly as if the dog were an ordinary moviegoer** — When the usherette expresses amazement at the dog's crying, the man calmly replies that it was a very sad film and later comments on the book, showing he views the dog's behavior as understandable except for the book contrast.

3. **A) A cinema worker who guides people to their seats** — In the context of a cinema, the usherette shows the man and his dog to their places (seats) in the stalls.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Guided Summary: A Surprise at the Cinema):**

A man took his dog to the cinema, bought two tickets, and sat in the stalls. The usherette was amazed because the dog watched the film closely and even cried like a baby when the hero died. After the film, she told the man it was the most amazing thing she had ever seen. The man agreed it was surprising, adding that the dog had actually hated the book.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: Books vs. Films):**

In my opinion, books are usually better than films. When you read a book, you can use your imagination to picture the characters and places in your own way. Books also give more details about a character's thoughts and feelings. However, films are sometimes more exciting because of music, sound effects, and strong acting, just like the sad film in the story. Still, reading the book first helps you enjoy and understand the story much better.


---


## Passage 34: Tom's Diary (`raw1_034` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **boring**

2. **nurse**

3. **India**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **A) French every morning and dance at night** — In his Monday entry, Tom writes that everything is changing and they now have French every morning and dance at night.

2. **C) At the Mayfair Hotel** — On Tuesday, Tom says he met Anna at the coffee bar and she is staying at the Mayfair Hotel just near the Bailey School.

3. **B) It was very good** — On Wednesday, Tom writes that he and his friend were at the folk club that evening and describes it as "very good."


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — Tom says he is studying Endgame, a play by Samuel Beckett, and describes it as very boring.

2. **True** — On Tuesday, Tom writes that he met Anna at the coffee bar and says, "I want to see Anna again!"


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **False** — Tom writes on Monday that they have French every morning and dance at night, not dance classes every morning.

2. **False** — Tom describes studying 'Endgame' by Samuel Beckett as "very boring."

3. **True** — In the entry for 26 Tuesday, Tom writes that in the evening he and his friend were at the coffee bar.

4. **True** — Tom describes Anna as a beautiful American nurse and writes, "I want to see Anna again!"

5. **She is from America (the United States).** — Tom describes Anna as "a beautiful American nurse."

6. **It is just near the Bailey School.** — Tom writes that Anna is staying "at the Mayfair Hotel just near the Bailey School."

7. **He doesn't like watching TV every night.** — On Wednesday, Tom writes, "My friend likes TV. I don't. Not every night!"

8. **It was very good.** — Tom writes, "We were at the folk club this evening - very good."

9. **'The Jewel in the Crown' (by Paul Scott).** — Tom writes, "I'm reading a fantastic book about India called 'The Jewel in the Crown' by Paul Scott."


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **D) He prefers social outings and good books over watching TV every night** — Tom finds his class play boring and dislikes watching TV every night, whereas he enjoys meeting Anna, going to the folk club, and reading his book about India.

2. **B) Three days of Tom's school life and free-time activities** — The passage is a set of three short diary entries written by Tom describing his school schedule, social life, and reading over three days in November 1996.

3. **A) His morning and evening class routine has become busier** — Tom writes that there were no classes in the mornings before, and now they have French every morning and dance at night, so his daily timetable has changed.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Guided Diary Entry: Tom's Thursday):**

Thursday, November 28th: Today I had another busy French class in the morning, and then I practiced my presentation in the afternoon. In the evening, I met Anna for tea near the Mayfair Hotel, and we talked about America and my school. Later, I went back to my room and read two more chapters of my book about India before going to sleep.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: Keeping a Diary):**

Keeping a diary is very useful for students. First, it helps you remember important events, homework, and special moments with friends. Second, writing a few sentences every evening is a great way to practice English and learn how to express your feelings clearly. Finally, a diary lets you look back months later and see how your life has changed. Even five minutes of writing before bed can make you a better writer.


---


## Passage 35: Elvis Presley (`raw1_035` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **twin**

2. **lorry driver / driver**

3. **1955**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **B) They were poor, kind, and loving** — The passage states that Elvis grew up in a poor home, but his parents, Vernon and Gladys Presley, were kind and loving.

2. **A) He noticed that Elvis was a talented singer** — Sam Phillips owned Sun Records and was the first person to realise that Elvis was a good singer.

3. **D) Many people believe he took too many drugs and pills** — The passage ends by stating that Elvis died on August 16, 1977, and many people say he died because he took too many drugs and pills.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — Before becoming a famous singer, Elvis worked in cinemas, supermarkets, and as a lorry driver.

2. **True** — The passage states that Elvis won a lot of gold discs in his life.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **False** — The passage states that his parents, Vernon and Gladys Presley, were "kind and loving."

2. **No Information** — The passage mentions that his twin brother died at birth, but it does not say whether Elvis had any other brothers or sisters or whether he was the youngest child.

3. **No Information** — The passage states that he grew up in a poor home and worked in different jobs after leaving school, but it does not specify his reason for taking those jobs.

4. **True** — After mentioning his work as a lorry driver, the passage says, "This was the job he liked most."

5. **True** — The passage lists Sam Phillips as one of the two people who helped Elvis in his career and says he was the first to realise Elvis was a good singer.

6. **False** — Sam Phillips had the record company Sun Records; Tom Parker became Elvis's manager in 1955, not the manager of Sun Records.

7. **False** — Elvis was born on January 8, 1935 and died on August 16, 1977 (at age 42), which is in August (summer), not on a winter day.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **C) The life and music career of Elvis Presley** — The passage gives a brief overview of Elvis Presley's birth, family, early jobs, rise to fame with the help of Sam Phillips and Tom Parker, success, and death.

2. **B) understand or notice** — In the passage, Sam Phillips "realised" Elvis was a good singer, meaning he recognized or understood Elvis's talent.

3. **A) Sam Phillips discovered Elvis's talent, while Tom Parker managed him to stardom** — Sam Phillips discovered Elvis's singing talent first, and Tom Parker later managed him starting in 1955 and made him a famous star, showing both men played key roles at different stages.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Summary: The Life of Elvis Presley):**

Elvis Presley was born in Mississippi in 1935 to poor but loving parents. After leaving school, he worked in cinemas, supermarkets, and as a lorry driver, which was his favorite job. Later, Sam Phillips of Sun Records recognized his singing talent, and Tom Parker became his manager in 1955, turning him into a rock and roll star. Although Elvis won many gold discs, he died in 1977, reportedly from taking too many drugs and pills.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: The Price of Fame):**

Becoming famous at a young age brings both advantages and serious challenges. On the positive side, young stars can earn a lot of money, travel the world, and follow their dreams early in life. However, fame also creates great pressure, and young people may lose their privacy or make unhealthy choices, as happened to Elvis Presley late in his life. Therefore, famous young people need strong, caring support from family and honest friends to stay safe and happy.


---


## Passage 36: Going to Britain (`raw1_036` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **pence**

2. **stamps**

3. **coaches**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **C) A 'bed and breakfast', youth hostels, or campsites** — The passage says hotels are very expensive in Britain, and suggests a 'bed and breakfast' in someone's home, youth hostels, or campsites as cheaper options.

2. **A) Learn about how it works before using it** — The passage advises that the London underground is not easy to use, so you should learn about it before using it, and you can get information in all London stations.

3. **D) Indian and Chinese restaurants** — According to the passage, Indian and Chinese restaurants serve good meals that are cheap, whereas pubs and regular restaurants are often expensive and fast food is not always very good.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — The passage says not all banks change foreign money, though you can usually find at least one bank in each town that does.

2. **True** — The passage states that trains are quite good and coaches are cheaper than trains.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **foreign money** — The "Money" section says: "Not all banks change foreign money, but you can usually find at least one bank in each town that will do it."

2. **youth hostel, campsite** — Under "Where To Stay," after mentioning 'bed and breakfast' places, the text says: "There are also youth hostels and campsites in many places."

3. **underground (the underground)** — The "Getting Around" section explains that the London underground is not easy to use and you can get information about it in all London stations.

4. **fish and chip shops, hamburger shops** — The "Eating Out" section lists "Fast food shops - fish and chip shops, hamburger shops".

5. **At information centres or tourist offices.** — In "Where To Stay," the passage states: "Information centres or tourist offices can help you to find these."

6. **a) under 24; c) travelling with a family** — The passage states: "If you are under 24 or over 65, or if you are travelling with a family, ask about 'railcards' for cheaper fares on the train."

7. **At Indian and Chinese restaurants.** — Under "Eating Out," the text says: "But Indian and Chinese restaurants serve good meals, which are cheap."

8. **Often inside a small shop.** — Under "Writing Home," the passage states that nearly every village has a post office, and "Often it is inside a small shop."


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **B) Practical travel advice for visitors to Britain on a budget** — Each section gives practical, budget-friendly tips for visitors to Britain covering currency, accommodation, postage, transport, and food.

2. **D) ticket prices** — In the context of getting cheaper train fares with a railcard, "fares" means the money you pay for a train ticket/journey.

3. **A) An Indian or Chinese restaurant** — A person who wants to save money without sacrificing food quality should avoid expensive restaurants/pubs and lower-quality fast food, and instead choose Indian or Chinese restaurants.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Guided Summary: Budget Travel Tips for Britain):**

Visitors to Britain can save money by choosing cheaper alternatives to expensive hotels and restaurants. For accommodation, tourist offices can help them find a bed and breakfast, youth hostel, or campsite. For travel, coaches cost less than trains, and young, senior, or family travelers can buy railcards for cheaper train fares. In London, visitors should learn about the underground at stations before using it. Finally, Indian and Chinese restaurants offer good, affordable meals.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: Planning a Trip Abroad):**

When visiting a foreign country, I think the most important thing to plan carefully is transportation and accommodation. First, if you know how to use local buses, trains, or the underground, you will save both time and money while exploring the city. Second, finding a safe and affordable place to stay, such as a youth hostel or a small guesthouse, helps you stay within your budget. Good planning before a trip reduces stress and lets you enjoy the journey much more.


---


## Passage 37: Transportation in Turkey (`raw1_037` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **90 percent / 90%**

2. **train wagons / wagons**

3. **coal**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **C) 75 percent** — The passage states that highway vehicles carry 75 percent of the cargo in Turkey.

2. **B) 50 tons** — According to the passage, one train wagon can carry 50 tons of cargo, compared to 10 tons for one truck.

3. **A) They use cheaper fuel and last longer.** — The speaker explains that trains use coal, which is cheaper than petrol, and train wagons can be used for more years than trucks.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — The passage states that trains carry 25 percent of the cargo in Turkey while highway vehicles carry 75 percent.

2. **False** — The passage explicitly states that a train wagon can be used for more years than a truck.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **Highway vehicles carry more passengers (90% compared to 5% by trains).** — The first paragraph states that highway vehicles transport 90% of the passengers, while trains carry only 5%.

2. **Trains (railway vehicles) carry less cargo (25% compared to 75% by highway vehicles).** — The first paragraph states that highway vehicles transport 75% of the cargo, whereas trains carry 25%.

3. **b) Truck (T) — implied $24,000 per truck from the 150-truck total ($3,600,000), though the single-unit line prints $30,000.** — The single-unit lines print $30,000 for both a train wagon and a truck, but 150 trucks costing $3,600,000 implies $24,000 per truck compared with $30,000 per train wagon.

4. **a) Train wagon (W) — $30,000 compared to an implied $24,000 per truck from the 150-truck total.** — While both single-unit lines print $30,000, the 150-truck total ($3,600,000) implies $24,000 per truck, making a single train wagon ($30,000) more expensive per unit.

5. **a) Train wagon (W)** — One train wagon can carry 50 tons, whereas one truck can carry 10 tons.

6. **b) Truck (T)** — One truck can carry only 10 tons, compared to 50 tons for one train wagon.

7. **a) Train wagon (W)** — The passage states: "We can use a train wagon for more years than we can use a truck."

8. **Coal is cheaper than petrol.** — The final paragraph states: "We all know petrol is more expensive than coal."

9. **A train is more economical than a truck.** — The last sentence concludes: "That's why trains are more economical than trucks."

10. **A train wagon can carry more cargo (50 tons vs. 10 tons), a train costs less overall for large cargo (1 locomotive + 30 wagons vs. 150 trucks), train wagons last for more years, and trains use coal, which is cheaper than petrol.** — These four advantages are presented in paragraphs 2 through 5 of the passage.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **C) To compare the costs and efficiency of railway and highway transport** — The passage compares passenger/cargo shares, vehicle capacities, purchase costs, lifespan, and fuel costs to show why railway transport is more economical.

2. **B) Transporting 1500 tons of cargo by train requires a smaller total vehicle cost than by truck.** — Since 150 trucks are needed for 1500 tons of cargo and they cost $3,600,000 together ($24,000 each on average), the 30-wagon train with a locomotive ($2,900,000) costs less for the same 1500 tons.

3. **D) cost-effective and money-saving** — In this context, 'economical' means costing less money to buy, maintain, and fuel over time.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Guided Summary: Trains vs. Trucks):**

In Turkey, highway vehicles carry 90% of passengers and 75% of cargo, while trains carry much less. However, railway transportation is more economical for large amounts of cargo. One train wagon carries 50 tons, so 1500 tons require only 30 wagons and a locomotive instead of 150 trucks. Also, train wagons last longer and trains use coal, which is cheaper than petrol.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: Investing in Railways):**

In my opinion, countries should invest more in railway transportation, especially for carrying heavy cargo. As the passage shows, trains are more economical because one wagon carries five times as much as a truck and lasts for more years. Although highways are useful for daily travel, having fewer trucks on the roads also reduces traffic and accidents. Therefore, expanding railways is a smart choice for both the economy and road safety.

## Passage 38: Amy's Restaurant (`raw1_038` · `A1`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **students**

2. **reasonable**

3. **special**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **B) In Greenwich Village** — The passage says Amy's Restaurant is a good place to eat in Greenwich Village.

2. **C) Tomato soup** — The special includes fish or meat, vegetables, a mixed salad, bread and butter, dessert, and coffee or tea—not soup.

3. **D) All menu items give good value for the price.** — The passage says the special and the other items on Amy's menu are always excellent for the prices.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — The passage directly states that the service at Amy's Restaurant is fast.

2. **Not Given** — The passage mentions lunch and dinner, and that there is a different special every day, but it does not state whether the restaurant is open 24 hours.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **You can eat at Amy's Restaurant in Greenwich Village.** — The first sentence states that Amy's Restaurant is a good place to eat in Greenwich Village.

2. **Amy's Restaurant is a clean place with fast service and reasonable prices where students can enjoy lunch and dinner.** — Sentences 2 and 3 describe Amy's Restaurant as a perfect place for students that is clean, has fast service, and offers reasonable prices.

3. **It is $3.45.** — The passage states that the daily special costs $3.45.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **A) Amy's Restaurant offers good, affordable meals for students.** — The passage describes the restaurant's location, cleanliness, fast service, reasonable prices, and daily special, showing why it is a great place to eat, especially for students.

2. **C) Students probably like places that are quick and inexpensive.** — Because the passage mentions fast service, reasonable prices, and a full meal for $3.45, we can infer that students eat there because they want a quick, affordable, and complete meal.

3. **B) fair and affordable** — In this passage, 'reasonable' describes prices that are fair and not too expensive for students.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Guided Response: Recommending Amy's Restaurant):**

Amy's Restaurant is a clean and affordable place in Greenwich Village. It is great for students who want lunch or dinner with fast service. Every day, the restaurant offers a special meal for $3.45. This meal includes meat or fish, vegetables, a mixed salad, bread and butter, dessert, and tea or coffee. All the food on the menu is very good for the price.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: My Favorite Student Cafe):**

My favorite place to eat near my school is a small cafe called Green Plate. Like Amy's Restaurant, it is very clean and the service is fast, so students do not have to wait long between classes. The prices are reasonable, and a warm lunch with soup, rice, salad, and tea costs very little. I like going there with my friends because the food is fresh and the staff are always friendly.

## Passage 39: Small Cars (`raw1_039` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **100 kilometers / 100 km**

2. **electricity**

3. **pollution**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **B) Always very crowded** — The passage states that the streets are always very crowded with cars, buses, and trucks.

2. **A) You save petrol and spend less money.** — Small cars are more economical because drivers can save petrol and spend less money.

3. **C) They will help reduce pollution in cities.** — The second paragraph explains that future small cars will probably use electricity, resulting in less pollution in cities.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — The passage says small cars are safer because they cannot go more than 100 kilometers per hour.

2. **True** — The passage states that in the future, a lot of people will probably use small cars.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **1) They are cheaper. 2) They are more economical. 3) They are safer.** — Paragraph 1 states that small cars are cheaper, more economical, and safer.

2. **1) You can save petrol. 2) You spend less money.** — Paragraph 1 explains: "they are more economical because you can save petrol and you spend less money."

3. **Sample answer: I would rather have a small car because it saves petrol, costs less money, and is easy to park on crowded streets.** — This is an open-ended personal preference question based on the advantages discussed in the passage.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **C) The benefits of small cars today and in the future** — The passage lists the current advantages of small cars (cheaper, economical, safer) and predicts their future use with electricity to reduce city pollution.

2. **B) Petrol-powered cars contribute to air pollution in cities.** — Since the passage says electric small cars will mean 'less pollution in cities', it implies that current petrol-using vehicles cause pollution in cities.

3. **D) prefer to** — 'Would rather' expresses a preference, meaning many people prefer to use small cars.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Guided Summary: Why Choose Small Cars?):**

Because city streets are very crowded today, many people prefer small cars. Small cars are cheaper to buy and more economical because drivers save petrol and spend less money. They are also safer since their speed cannot go over 100 kilometers per hour. In the future, small cars will probably become even more popular and run on electricity, which will help reduce pollution in cities.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: Small vs. Big Cars):**

I would rather drive a small car in a big city because it is much more practical. First, city streets are crowded and parking spaces are hard to find, so a small car makes daily driving easier. Second, small cars save petrol and money, which is very important for people with a tight budget. Finally, electric small cars help protect the environment by causing less pollution. For these reasons, small cars are a smart choice for city life.

## Passage 40: Home Sweet Home (`raw1_040` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **60 percent / 60%**

2. **flats**

3. **repairs**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **C) Most British people do not live in rented housing.** — The passage states that 60 percent of the population does not live in rented housing, meaning they own or live in non-rented homes.

2. **A) In the north of England and in Scotland** — According to the second paragraph, prices are especially high in the southeast of England, whereas in the north and in Scotland, prices are quite low.

3. **B) They are less expensive to buy.** — Young people often buy old houses because they are not so expensive, even though they require repairs and decoration.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — The passage notes that many young people do not have enough money to buy a house, so they live in small flats or in their parents' house, even when they are married.

2. **False** — The passage states that people who buy old houses have to spend a lot of time and money on repairs and decoration.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **A house is very important for many British people.** — The first sentence states: "For many British people, a house is the most important thing in their lives."

2. **40% of the population lives in rented housing.** — Because 60% of the population does not live in rented housing, 40% (100% - 60%) lives in rented housing.

3. **No, they cannot.** — The passage states that prices are going up all the time and many young people do not have enough money to buy a house.

4. **Because house prices are going up all the time and many young people have not got enough money.** — Paragraph 1 directly gives rising prices and lack of enough money as the reasons.

5. **They live in small flats or in their parents' house (even when they are married).** — The last sentence of paragraph 1 states where young people live when they cannot afford a house.

6. **House prices are especially high in the southeast of England, whereas in the north (and in Scotland) prices are quite low.** — Paragraph 2 contrasts the high prices in the southeast of England with the quite low prices in the north and Scotland.

7. **Because old houses are not so expensive.** — Paragraph 2 states: "Young people often buy old houses, perhaps more than a hundred years old because they are not so expensive."

8. **They have to spend a lot of time and money on repairs and decoration.** — The final sentence states that after buying old houses, they must spend a lot of time and money on repairs and decoration.

9. **(Many) British people** — The previous sentences discuss 'many British people' and '60% of the population', so 'They' refers to British people who want their own houses.

10. **Many young people (who have not got enough money to buy a house)** — The preceding clause mentions 'many young people have not got enough money to buy a house', which 'They' refers back to.

11. **Old houses** — In "Young people often buy old houses, perhaps more than a hundred years old because they are not so expensive," 'they' refers to 'old houses'.

12. **Young people (who buy old houses)** — In "But they have to spend a lot of time and money on repairs and decoration," 'they' refers to the young people who buy old houses.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **B) Home ownership and housing challenges for young people in Britain** — The passage explains that British people value owning a house, why young people struggle with rising prices, how prices differ by region, and why they buy old houses.

2. **D) 40%** — Since 60% of the population does not live in rented housing, the remaining 40% lives in rented housing.

3. **A) increasing** — 'Going up' in the context of house prices means increasing or becoming higher.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Guided Summary: Housing in Britain):**

Many British people want to own a house, and 60% of the population does not live in rented homes. However, rising prices make it hard for young people to buy one, so many live in small flats or with their parents. Because prices are very high in the southeast of England, young people often buy old houses, which are cheaper, even though they need expensive repairs and decoration.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: Buying an Old House):**

I think buying an old house can be a good idea for young people if the price is low. First, a cheaper house helps them have their own home instead of paying rent every month. Second, they can slowly repair and decorate it according to their own taste. However, they should check the building carefully before buying, because big repairs can cost a lot of time and money. Overall, an old house is a smart option with good planning.


---


## Passage 41: A Meeting (`raw1_041` · `A1`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **party**

2. **Geneva**

3. **campus**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **C) The host of the party** — At the start of the conversation, the host introduces Dr. Keller and Mr. Hakada to each other.

2. **B) Toronto** — Mr. Hakada asks Dr. Keller if she is going to attend the World Health Conference in Toronto this weekend.

3. **D) To visit the university offices in Tokyo** — Mr. Hakada asks Dr. Keller if she would like to see the UN University offices when she comes to Tokyo.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — Dr. Keller tells Mr. Hakada that she knows very little about the UN University.

2. **Not Given** — The dialogue mentions she is at a UN party and must return to Geneva, but it does not say where she normally works or lives.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **He is the director of the United Nations University in Tokyo.** — The host introduces Mr. Hakada as "the director of the United Nations University in Tokyo."

2. **She works for the World Health Organisation.** — The host says, "this is Dr. Keller. She works for the World Health Organisation."

3. **Because she has to return to Geneva on Friday.** — Dr. Keller replies, "No. I have to return to Geneva on Friday."

4. **It is in Tokyo.** — The host states that Mr. Hakada is the director of the United Nations University in Tokyo, and Mr. Hakada says they have offices in Tokyo.

5. **They do a lot of research.** — Mr. Hakada says, "We do a lot of research at the UN University."

6. **No, it doesn't. It has offices in Tokyo instead.** — Mr. Hakada says, "We do not have a campus but we have offices in Tokyo."

7. **No, she hasn't.** — When Mr. Hakada asks if she has visited the offices, Dr. Keller answers, "No, I haven't."


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **C) At a party at the United Nations** — The first sentence states that Dr. Keller is at a party at the United Nations.

2. **A) She will be in Geneva instead of Toronto during the weekend.** — Dr. Keller says she cannot attend the weekend conference in Toronto because she has to return to Geneva on Friday.

3. **B) go to** — In "Are you going to attend the World Health Conference in Toronto this weekend?", "attend" means to go to or take part in an event.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Guided Summary: Two Professionals Meet):**

Dr. Keller and Mr. Hakada meet at a party at the United Nations. Dr. Keller works for the World Health Organisation, and Mr. Hakada is the director of the UN University in Tokyo. Dr. Keller cannot attend the conference in Toronto because she must return to Geneva on Friday. Mr. Hakada tells her about the research at the university and invites her to visit their offices in Tokyo.


**Task 2 (Meeting New People):**

When I meet someone new at a school or work event, I usually smile and say hello first. I tell them my name and ask about their job or studies. For example, I can ask where they work or what they are interested in. It is also nice to ask if they have visited my city before. Being polite and listening carefully helps me make new friends and feel comfortable at big events.

## Passage 42: Laura (`raw1_042` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **customs officer**

2. **history**

3. **girl**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **B) Ireland** — The passage states that Laura was born in Bristol, and her parents originally came from Ireland.

2. **C) After she finished university and joined another group** — After she finished university, Laura joined another group and began to earn money with her performances and songs.

3. **D) Move from Britain to Los Angeles** — At the end of the passage, Laura and Tony decided a few days ago to leave Britain and move to Los Angeles.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — The passage explicitly states that Laura is an only child.

2. **False** — When they got married, Laura and Tony rented a farmhouse in a suburb of Bristol rather than buying one.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **She went to Bristol University when she was 18 (after leaving school).** — The passage states, "After leaving school when she was 18, Laura went to Bristol University to study history and economics."

2. **No, she wrote it while she was at university.** — While at university, she joined a music group and "It was at this time that she wrote her famous song 'The Price of Peace'."

3. **She was 21.** — The text says, "When she was 21, she married Tony Harper..."

4. **They have two children (a boy and a girl).** — The passage states, "They have two children - a boy and a girl."

5. **It is in a suburb of Bristol.** — They "rented a beautiful farmhouse in a suburb of Bristol" and "They are still there".

6. **Yes, they decided to leave Britain and move to Los Angeles only a few days ago.** — The last sentence says, "only a few days ago they decided to leave Britain and move to Los Angeles."

7. **Bristol University** — In the preceding sentence, Laura went to Bristol University, so "there" refers to Bristol University.

8. **Tony Harper** — The relative pronoun "who" immediately follows Tony Harper and describes him as a student at that time.

9. **Has Laura ever lived in Ireland? No, she hasn't. (Her parents came from Ireland, but she was born and grew up in Bristol.)** — Laura was born in Bristol in 1953, grew up in its suburbs, attended school and university there, and still lives in a Bristol suburb.

10. **How long has Laura lived in Bristol? She has lived in Bristol all her life (since she was born in 1953).** — She was born in Bristol in 1953, grew up and studied there, rented a house in a suburb of Bristol, and is still there.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **C) The life and career of singer Laura Denison** — The text covers Laura's birth, education, musical career, marriage, and upcoming move to Los Angeles.

2. **B) She married Tony Harper around the time she finished university.** — Laura went to Bristol University at age 18 and stayed for three years, finishing around age 21, which is the same age she married Tony Harper.

3. **A) a residential area outside the city center** — In "grew up in the suburbs of Bristol" and "rented a beautiful farmhouse in a suburb of Bristol," a suburb is a residential area just outside the center of a city.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Biography Summary: Laura Denison):**

Laura Denison is a famous singer who was born in Bristol in 1953. After attending schools in Bristol, she studied history and economics at Bristol University for three years. There, she joined a music group, began singing, and wrote her hit song 'The Price of Peace'. After university, she started earning money from her music. At 21, she married Tony Harper, and they now have two children and are planning to move from Bristol to Los Angeles.


**Task 2 (Moving to a New Country):**

Moving to a new city or country is a big decision with both advantages and disadvantages. On the positive side, living in a new place like Los Angeles can bring better job opportunities and exciting new experiences. Children can also learn about different cultures and meet new friends. However, leaving your hometown can be difficult because you have to say goodbye to old friends and familiar places. In my opinion, moving abroad is a great idea if a family has a good plan and wants to build a better future together.


---


## Passage 43: Letter from Wendy (`raw1_043` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **Airport**

2. **bus tour**

3. **housework**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **C) She is very busy guiding tourists and doing housework.** — Wendy says August is a bad time to get away because she is very busy with tourists, and on Sunday she has to do housework.

2. **A) London** — Wendy asks Janet, "Wouldn't you like to come to London for a day or two instead?... What about next week?"

3. **D) In October** — Wendy writes, "But I think I can visit you in October."


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — Wendy says that when the Japanese tourists arrive, she has to take them to their hotel.

2. **Not Given** — The letter invites Janet to London next week, but it never states whether Janet has visited Wendy in London in the past.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **London; Torquay** — Wendy invites Janet to come to London instead, and Janet invited Wendy to come down to Torquay.

2. **a group of Japanese tourists** — Wendy writes, "At the moment, I'm waiting for a group of Japanese tourists. I'm actually writing this letter at Heathrow Airport!"

3. **True** — Wendy says she has to take the tourists on a bus tour "tomorrow" and she is "free on Sunday," which means tomorrow is Saturday and she wrote the letter on Friday.

4. **True** — She meets tourists at the airport, takes them to their hotel, and takes them on a bus tour of the city.

5. **False** — Wendy is very busy in August, not October; she thinks she can visit Janet in October.

6. **False** — Wendy cannot visit Janet now and plans to visit in October; instead, she invites Janet to London next week.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **B) To decline an invitation politely and suggest another plan** — Wendy writes to thank Janet for the invitation, explain why she cannot visit Torquay now, and invite Janet to London instead.

2. **D) Wendy expects to still be busy with tourists next week.** — Wendy asks Janet to come to London "for a day or two" next week and jokes, "You can help me with the tourists!"

3. **A) leave for a short break or trip** — In "August is a bad time for me to get away," "get away" means to leave home or work for a short holiday or break.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Guided Reply: Janet's Letter to Wendy):**

Dear Wendy,

Thank you for your letter! I understand that August is a very busy month for you with all the tourists in London. It would be wonderful to see you in October instead. I would also love to visit London next week for a couple of days. I'm free from Tuesday to Thursday, and it sounds fun to help you with the tour! Please let me know which days work best for you.

Best wishes,
Janet


**Task 2 (Working as a Tourist Guide):**

Working as a tourist guide sounds like an exciting and interesting job. First, tourist guides meet people from many different countries and learn about their cultures every day. They also spend time outdoors and visit famous places in the city instead of sitting in an office all day. However, this job can also be very tiring, especially in busy summer months like August when guides have to work on weekends. Overall, I would enjoy being a tourist guide because I love traveling, speaking English, and helping visitors have a good time.


---


## Passage 44: Education at Home (1) (`raw1_044` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **London**

2. **teachers**

3. **languages**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **A) On a Wednesday morning** — The passage states, "Our reporter visited them on a Wednesday morning."

2. **C) The teachers are weak and the students behave badly.** — Marian says she does not think the teachers are good, some students cannot read, and the children fight, smoke, and drink.

3. **B) The piano** — Ginny tells the reporter, "I play the piano and Donald plays the guitar."


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — The passage states that the family is large, with two girls and three boys, which makes five children.

2. **Not Given** — The passage mentions that Ginny is one of the daughters and Donald plays the guitar, but it never tells us their ages.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **She is a reporter.** — The text introduces her as "Our reporter, Jean Anderson".

2. **They have five children (two girls and three boys).** — The passage states, "The family is large - two girls and three boys."

3. **Because they don't go to school; they study at home with their parents.** — The text explains, "The children don't go to school. Their parents, Marian and Roger, teach them at home."

4. **Their parents, Marian and Roger, teach them.** — The passage says, "Their parents, Marian and Roger, teach them at home."

5. **They study at home.** — The passage says their parents "teach them at home."

6. **False** — Marian Richardson says, "My husband and I don't like the schools in our town. We don't think the teachers are very good."

7. **False** — Marian says it is the children at the local schools who fight, smoke, and drink, not her own children.

8. **Some of them can't read.** — Marian says, "Some children at these schools can't read."

9. **True** — Ginny says, "We learn more at home. I think we're lucky."

10. **She thinks they are very bad.** — Ginny says, "The schools here are very bad."


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **D) A family that chooses to educate their children at home** — The article describes the Richardson family, why the parents choose to teach their five children at home, and how their daughter Ginny feels about it.

2. **A) Wednesday morning is normally a school day for children in England.** — When the reporter visited on a Wednesday morning—a normal school day—the children were at home playing music and studying instead of attending school.

3. **C) pleased and fortunate** — Ginny says her parents are very good at music and languages, they learn more at home, and therefore "I think we're lucky."


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Guided Summary: The Richardson Family):**

Marian and Roger Richardson have five children, and they teach all of them at home near London instead of sending them to school. Marian explains that she and Roger, who are both teachers, do not like the local schools because the teachers are not very good and students fight, smoke, and drink there. Her daughter Ginny agrees that the local schools are bad and feels lucky to learn music and other subjects at home.


**Task 2 (Home Education vs. School):**

In my opinion, going to a normal school is usually better than studying at home, although home education has some advantages. At school, children learn to make friends, work in teams, and talk to many different people. Most parents are not trained teachers and may not know every subject well, such as science or mathematics. However, if a local school is very unsafe or has bad teachers, studying at home with educated parents like the Richardsons can be a good solution. For most children, though, school life helps them grow both academically and socially.


---


## Passage 45: Education at Home (2) (`raw1_045` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **4000 / 4,000 / four thousand**

2. **mathematics / math / maths**

3. **boring**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **C) At home with their mother** — The passage states that six-year-old Stephen and eight-year-old Jason study at home with their mother.

2. **B) By writing shopping lists and paying at the shops** — Eve says the children write shopping lists for her and then go shopping with her and pay for the things they buy.

3. **D) Most are interested, and some are considering doing the same.** — Eve mentions that most of their friends are very interested, and some are thinking about teaching their children at home too.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — Eve and James take their children to the library, museums, concerts, swimming pools, and gymnastics, believing these activities are very important.

2. **Not Given** — The passage gives James's age (29) and Eve's age (27) and says they got diplomas from school, but it does not mention what jobs they do.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **True** — The first paragraph states that the Tanner family is from Hertfordshire.

2. **False** — James says, "Both Eve and I think we got nothing from school - only a diploma," which shows they did attend school.

3. **True** — The third paragraph states: "Some parents who are teaching their children at home have a study programme, but the Tanners don't."

4. **False** — Eve says she also teaches them mathematics in addition to reading and writing.

5. **False** — Eve says most friends are interested and only some friends are thinking about teaching their children at home.

6. **Four (Eve, James, and their two sons Stephen and Jason).** — The first paragraph names Eve Tanner, her husband James, and their two children, Stephen and Jason.

7. **Because they think these activities are very important.** — The fourth paragraph states that they take their children to the library, museums, concerts, swimming pools, and gymnastics because "They think these activities are very important."


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **A) To show how one family successfully educates their children at home** — The passage mainly describes how the Tanner family educates their two sons at home without a formal study programme, using everyday tasks and outside activities.

2. **C) They prefer flexible, real-life learning over a formal curriculum.** — Because the Tanners have no fixed study programme and involve the children in real-life shopping and outings, their approach is flexible and practical rather than strict.

3. **B) Many different kinds of things** — "All sorts of things" means many different kinds or varieties of books and reading materials.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Summarize the Tanners' Home Education):**

Eve and James Tanner educate their two young sons, Stephen and Jason, at home in Hertfordshire because they found their own school years boring. Instead of following a strict study programme, Eve teaches the boys reading, writing, and mathematics through story books and everyday tasks like writing shopping lists and paying at shops. The parents also regularly take their children to libraries, museums, concerts, and sports activities.


**Task 2 (Home Education vs. Regular School):**

In my opinion, studying at home has both advantages and disadvantages, so regular school is better for most children. At home, parents can give personal attention and make learning fun, like the Tanners do. However, schools help children make many friends and learn from different teachers. Students also learn how to work in groups and follow schedules. For these reasons, I think going to school with friends is usually better than studying only at home.

## Passage 46: John Lennon (`raw1_046` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **Quarrymen / the Quarrymen**

2. **Ringo Starr**

3. **Central Park**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **C) In Liverpool, England** — The passage states that Lennon was born on October 9, 1940, in Liverpool, England.

2. **B) 1958 to 1962** — Between 1958 and 1962, the Beatles played in night clubs in Liverpool and Hamburg, Germany.

3. **A) He acted in a film.** — In 1967, John Lennon acted in a film called How I Won The War.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — The passage says that John Lennon wrote many of the Beatles' songs and most of them became number one all over the world.

2. **False** — Lennon wrote his first book in 1964 and his second book a year after that, in 1965.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **a) the music of Little Richard** — The second paragraph says: "When he was a child, he listened to the music of rock and roll singers like Elvis Presley, Jerry Lee Lewis and Little Richard."

2. **c) The Quarrymen** — The second paragraph states: "At 15, when he was in art school, Lennon started his first rock-group, the Quarrymen."

3. **c) with the song 'I Wanna Hold Your Hand'** — The third paragraph states: "Then the Beatles made a record called 'I Wanna Hold Your Hand' and had their first big success in Britain."

4. **b) Lennon wrote his second book** — The fifth paragraph says he wrote two books, "the first one in 1964 and the second one a year after that" (1965).

5. **d) Lennon had great success with his songs.** — The fourth paragraph says he wrote many of the Beatles' songs and most of them became number one all over the world. How I Won The War was a film, today's musicians still play his songs, and he died in New York.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **D) John Lennon was talented in several areas besides music.** — The passage explains that Lennon was not only a singer and songwriter with the Beatles, but also wrote two books and acted in a film.

2. **B) He expressed the thoughts and feelings of young people.** — Saying "many young people of the 1960's and 1970's thought he spoke for them" means young people felt he expressed their thoughts, feelings, and ideas.

3. **C) His music and memory remain influential many years after his death.** — The passage states that today's musicians still play Lennon's songs and thousands of people gather in Central Park every year to remember him, showing his lasting influence.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Biography Summary: John Lennon):**

John Lennon was born in Liverpool in 1940 and formed his first band, the Quarrymen, at age 15. The group later became the Beatles and achieved huge success with hit songs like 'I Wanna Hold Your Hand' and 'Please Please Me'. Lennon wrote many famous Beatles songs, published two books in 1964 and 1965, and acted in a film in 1967. After he was shot dead in New York in 1980, fans continue to honour his memory every year.


**Task 2 (A Musician I Admire):**

My favourite musician is Baris Manco because his songs are creative and meaningful. Like John Lennon, he wrote his own music and spoke to people of all ages. His songs tell interesting stories about everyday life, friendship, and culture. Many young musicians still play his songs today, and people remember him with love. I enjoy listening to his music because it always makes me feel happy and hopeful.
