## Passage 47: Guide to Good Eating (`raw1_047` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **suburb**

2. **six tables / 6 tables**

3. **12 pounds / twelve pounds**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **B) Only some pizza houses and a few hamburger cafes** — Before visiting the Rajah Restaurant, the writer thought there were only pizza houses and a few hamburger cafes in Cornwell.

2. **A) By bus and then on foot** — They got off the bus at Cornwell Central, walked down George Road to the post office, and then turned left into West Lane.

3. **C) Because the restaurant is small and only has six tables** — The writer tells readers to hurry because the restaurant only has six tables and cannot seat 14,000 people at once.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — The passage describes the Tandoori chicken as very tasty, the curries as hot and delicious, the wine as good and cheap, and the fruit salad as really exotic.

2. **Not Given** — The passage mentions that a friend took the writer out to dinner last Saturday, but it does not say the friend's name.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **a) Pizza houses, b) Hamburger cafes** — The first paragraph mentions "some pizza houses and a few hamburger cafes", and the rest of the passage describes The Rajah Restaurant.

2. **In a little street called West Lane (off George Road, near the post office).** — The second paragraph explains that after getting off the bus at Cornwell Central, you walk down George Road to the post office, turn left into West Lane, and walk a few metres.

3. **The writer (Tom Cousin) and a friend.** — The second paragraph says: "A friend took me out to dinner last Saturday."

4. **They had an Indian meal: Tandoori chicken, curries, wine, and fruit salad.** — The third paragraph lists Tandoori chicken, curries, wine, and fruit salad.

5. **True** — The first paragraph says: "It isn't big - about 14,000 people live there."

6. **False** — The second paragraph says: "We got off the bus at Cornwell Central", which shows they travelled by bus, not by car.

7. **False** — The third paragraph says: "there wasn't anyone there when we arrived."

8. **False** — The writer says the wine was "good and cheap" and the meal cost "only £12 for two", meaning it was inexpensive.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **C) To recommend a small and affordable Indian restaurant in Cornwell** — The passage is a positive restaurant review recommending a small Indian restaurant in Cornwell called The Rajah Restaurant.

2. **D) Unusual and from a distant or tropical place** — "Exotic" describes something unusual, interesting, and from a distant or tropical place, especially food or fruit.

3. **A) He expects the restaurant to become popular once people hear about it.** — When they arrived there wasn't anyone there, yet the writer thinks many of Cornwell's 14,000 residents will want to go once they hear about this great, inexpensive place.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Restaurant Visit Summary):**

Last Saturday, Tom Cousin went to a small Indian restaurant called The Rajah Restaurant on West Lane in Cornwell, a small suburb of Bristol. Although the restaurant had only six tables and was empty when they arrived, he and his friend enjoyed a delicious meal of Tandoori chicken, hot curries, cheap wine, and exotic fruit salad for only £12. He strongly recommends visiting it soon.


**Task 2 (Write a Short Restaurant Review):**

A great place to eat near my home is a small family cafe called Green Garden. It is on a quiet street behind the central library, so it is easy to walk there. The cafe is warm and clean, and the waiters are very friendly. They serve fresh homemade soup, grilled chicken, and delicious apple pie. The prices are quite cheap for students, and the food is always tasty. I always recommend this cafe to my friends.

## Passage 48: Radio Times Reader Holidays (`raw1_048` · `B1`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **149 pounds / £149**

2. **River Nile / Nile**

3. **Thursdays / Thursday**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **B) The 3-star San Pawl Hotel** — In Malta, guests stay with half board accommodation and English breakfast at the 3-star San Pawl Hotel.

2. **D) A bottle of wine in the room on arrival** — The Malta package includes a bottle of wine in the room on arrival, hotel entertainment, courier services, special trips, and a travel bag, whereas guided tours throughout the stay are listed for Egypt.

3. **C) At a supplement, in addition to weekly Heathrow flights** — The Malta holiday has weekly flights from Heathrow on Fridays, and Manchester flights are also available at a supplement.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — Both the Malta holiday and the Egypt holiday list a "superb travel bag" among what is included.

2. **False** — The brochure describes Egypt's weather as having a cloudless blue sky for most of the year.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **False** — The brochure states that £385 is the starting price for a full 7-day (one-week) tour in Egypt, not a two-week holiday.

2. **True** — The Malta holiday includes special trips to the Handicrafts Centre and spectacular excursions, while the Egypt holiday includes visits to Cairo and Alexandria plus trips on the River Nile and to the pyramids.

3. **False** — The Malta description explicitly notes "welcoming people (many speak English)", so English-only speakers will not easily have language problems.

4. **True** — The Egypt section states that the tour includes "Scheduled flights from Heathrow and Manchester".

5. **True** — The Malta section states: "Flights from Heathrow - every week on Friday from 3/11/95 to 29/3/96."


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **A) Malta focuses on relaxing winter sunshine and value stays, while Egypt highlights guided historical sightseeing.** — The brochure emphasizes Malta's winter sunshine and affordable 1-week and 2-week stays, while highlighting Egypt's ancient monuments, Nile trips, and guided sightseeing tours.

2. **C) Two weeks** — A "fortnight" means two weeks (14 nights), as shown by comparing £149 for a week and £179 for a fortnight.

3. **B) Greater or better than anything else; unmatched** — "Unparalleled" means having no equal or match, emphasizing how extraordinary and unique Egypt's history is.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Compare the Two Holiday Packages):**

The Radio Times brochure advertises two winter holiday packages to Malta and Egypt. The Malta trip starts at £149 for one week or £179 for a fortnight, offering half board at a 3-star hotel, local excursions, hotel entertainment, and Friday flights from Heathrow. In contrast, the 7-day tour to Egypt costs from £385 and features fully guided visits to Cairo, Alexandria, the River Nile, and the pyramids, with Thursday departures from Heathrow and Manchester.


**Task 2 (Choosing Your Ideal Winter Holiday):**

Between the two holiday options in the brochure, I would prefer to choose the 7-day tour to Egypt. Although the Malta holiday is much cheaper and lasts up to a fortnight, Egypt offers a truly unique cultural experience. Being able to see the famous pyramids, sail on the River Nile, and explore historic cities like Cairo and Alexandria sounds unforgettable. Moreover, having fully guided tours throughout the stay makes travelling in a foreign country much easier and more informative. Even though the weather in Malta is also warm and pleasant, exploring ancient history under a cloudless sky makes the Egypt tour my ideal choice.

## Passage 49: Seaford School & Bedgebury School (`raw1_049` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **lifeguard / a lifeguard**

2. **volleyball**

3. **different floors**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **B) 10 to 15 years old** — The script says Seaford School is for ages 10 to 15, from 25th July to 14th August.

2. **C) 55 kilometres from London** — Bedgebury School is located in a town 55 kilometres from London.

3. **A) Computers and riding** — Under 'Other Activities' for Seaford School, the script mentions computers and riding.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — The script states that Bedgebury School has a lake for watersports and a modern indoor sports centre.

2. **False** — The script says students at Bedgebury School sleep in simply furnished rooms with five beds, not private single rooms.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **False** — Bedgebury School is situated in a beautiful town 55 kms from London, not in London itself.

2. **True** — The passage describes Seaford School as a 'Summer School centre' running from 25 July to 14 August.

3. **False** — The text states that 'Boys and girls have rooms on different floors', so it is for both boys and girls.

4. **True** — The passage says that students sleep in simply furnished rooms with 5 beds.

5. **False** — Seaford School is '1 3/4 hours by train from London', so you can go there by train.

6. **It is on England's south coast.** — The text states: 'The seaside town of Seaford is on England's south coast.'

7. **Swimming, tennis, football, and riding.** — The school grounds have an open-air heated swimming pool, six tennis courts, and football fields, and riding is listed under 'Other Activities'.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **C) It combines the seaside with access to the countryside.** — The passage mentions that Seaford is a seaside town and offers 'an ideal combination of sea and country', making it great for exploring the South Downs and nearby towns.

2. **B) Care and watching over by adults** — At Bedgebury School, students are 'under the supervision of resident teachers', meaning teachers live on-site and look after the students.

3. **A) To advertise two residential or summer school programmes for young people** — Both school descriptions highlight sports facilities, outdoor activities, and nearby places to explore to attract young students and parents.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Comparing Two Schools):**

Seaford School and Bedgebury School both offer great summer and residential experiences near London. Seaford School is for students aged 10 to 15 and is on England's south coast, 115 kms from London. Students can enjoy swimming, tennis, football, riding, and computers. Bedgebury School is for ages 13 to 16 and sits 55 kms from London. It has playing fields, a lake for watersports, an indoor sports centre, and tennis courts, with five students sharing each room.


**Task 2 (My Preferred Summer School):**

If I could choose one of these schools, I would choose Bedgebury School. First, I am very interested in sports, and Bedgebury has superb facilities like a lake for watersports and a modern indoor sports centre. I would love to try volleyball and watersports with friends. Second, the school is only 55 kilometres from London and has interesting places nearby, such as cathedrals and country parks. Also, sharing a room with four other students sounds fun and helps you make new friends quickly.

## Passage 50: Letter from George (`raw1_050` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **camping holiday**

2. **outside lane**

3. **fireman**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **C) The painkillers made him sleepy.** — George explains that the painkillers made him sleepy when Tim visited him in the hospital.

2. **B) Midnight** — They left at about half-past five and thought they had plenty of time because the ferry did not leave until midnight.

3. **A) He looked down to get 60 pence from his coat pocket.** — George took his hand off the wheel to feel in his coat pocket for 60 pence and looked down at the money for a split second.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — The script says the traffic was very heavy and there was a lot of water on the road.

2. **False** — George says he thinks the crash from behind (the lorry) happened first, and it pushed their car into the coach.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **Because he had a car accident.** — George describes his first accident in the Dartford Tunnel and says he came home from the hospital last weekend.

2. **Tim.** — The letter is addressed 'Dear Tim,' and begins 'Thanks for your letter and for coming to the hospital.'

3. **His legs still hurt.** — George writes: 'My legs still hurt, but I'm walking round the garden as much as I can, so it's getting better.'

4. **France.** — George says they were 'looking forward to our camping holiday in France.'

5. **A camping holiday.** — He writes: 'we were all looking forward to our camping holiday in France.'

6. **By car (and they planned to take a ferry from Dover).** — George was driving with his hand on the wheel and they were heading to Dover to catch the midnight ferry.

7. **Nearly 8 o'clock.** — The letter states: 'In fact, we didn't get to the motorway until nearly 8 o'clock.'

8. **60p.** — George writes: 'I asked Anne for 60p to pay at the other end of the Tunnel.'

9. **A fireman.** — He writes: 'The next thing I saw was a bright light. A fireman was standing over me.'


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **B) He lost consciousness and could not see or remember anything.** — George lost consciousness right after the two crashes ('Then everything went black') and again after the doctor gave him an injection ('everything went black again').

2. **D) A very brief moment** — George says he looked down 'only for a split second', meaning a very short moment, yet it was long enough to miss the coach's brake lights.

3. **C) George was trapped inside the vehicle after the crash.** — The fireman told George, 'Lie still. We'll soon cut you free,' showing that George was trapped inside the damaged car.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Summarizing George's Accident):**

George wrote to Tim after coming home from the hospital. While driving with his family to Dover for a camping holiday in France, he entered the Dartford Tunnel at 9:15 in heavy traffic and wet weather. When he took his hand off the wheel to look for 60p in his pocket, he did not notice a coach ahead in time. His car skidded, and a lorry hit them from behind, pushing them into the coach until rescue workers cut him free.


**Task 2 (Staying Safe on the Road):**

Drivers can prevent accidents like George's by keeping their full attention on the road at all times. First, drivers should prepare toll money or change before starting a trip so they never need to search their pockets while driving. Second, people should never drive fast in heavy rain because wet roads make cars skid easily. Finally, if a driver feels tired after work, they should rest before a long journey or share the driving with another adult.


---


## Passage 51: Nottingham (`raw1_051` · `B1`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **three theatres / 3 theatres**

2. **heavy industry**

3. **golf course**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **A) It is near one of Britain's richest coalfields.** — The script states Nottingham is close to the centre of one of Britain's richest coalfields and is important for its many kinds of industry.

2. **D) Only 13,000 people** — The script states that Bangor has a population of only 13,000, compared with Nottingham's almost 300,000 inhabitants.

3. **C) A railway museum** — Near Bangor, visitors can see Penrhyn Castle, which has a railway museum.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — The script explicitly notes that even though Nottingham is an industrial city, it is not at all ugly or dirty.

2. **True** — According to the script, Trent Bridge is a first-class cricket ground in Nottingham.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **In Sherwood Forest (a short distance away from Nottingham).** — The passage mentions 'Sherwood Forest, the famous home of Robin Hood.'

2. **It is a small town (population of only 13,000) and it has little or no heavy industry.** — The text says: 'It is a small town with a population of only 13,000 and with little or no heavy industry. Therefore, it is still clean and quiet.'

3. **True** — The text states that in Bangor 'There is a theatre and a cinema' and 'an 18-hole golf course'.

4. **True** — The passage notes that Nottingham is an industrial city, 'but it is not at all ugly or dirty,' and describes its hill location, castle, parks, and cultural sites.

5. **False** — Besides two professional football clubs and a cricket ground, Nottingham also has the National Water Sports Centre in its large country park.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **C) Nottingham is much larger and more industrial than Bangor.** — Nottingham has nearly 300,000 inhabitants and many kinds of industry, whereas Bangor has only 13,000 people and little or no heavy industry. Both have a university, theatre, cinema, castle, and sports facilities.

2. **B) The museum is highly recommended and worth seeing.** — The author writes 'visitors should not miss the Museum of Welsh Antiquities', meaning it is so interesting and worthwhile that tourists should definitely go see it.

3. **A) Residents living in the city** — 'Inhabitants' refers to the people who live in a town or city ('almost 300,000 inhabitants').


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Comparing Nottingham and Bangor):**

Nottingham and Bangor are both British university towns with rich cultural and sports facilities, yet they differ greatly in size. Nottingham is a busy industrial and commercial city in the Midlands with nearly 300,000 inhabitants, famous for Sherwood Forest, its castle, three theatres, and top sports venues. In contrast, Bangor is a quiet, small town of 13,000 people in North Wales with almost no heavy industry, offering museums, a cathedral, a golf course, and nearby Penrhyn Castle.


**Task 2 (Big City vs. Small Town Living):**

If I had to live in one of these towns, I would prefer Bangor because of its peaceful environment. With a population of only 13,000 and little heavy industry, Bangor is clean and quiet, which makes daily life much less stressful than in a big city. Moreover, I enjoy nature and history, so walking in the famous public garden or visiting Penrhyn Castle and its railway museum at weekends would be wonderful. Finally, having a modern swimming pool and a university nearby keeps the town lively without feeling overcrowded.

## Passage 52: A Language Teacher's Personal Opinion (`raw1_052` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **airports**

2. **three months / 3 months**

3. **waiter**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **B) English** — The script gives an example of a Mexican businessman buying something from a Chinese businessman and speaking English.

2. **D) Awful and unrealistic** — The speaker is an English teacher who calls those advertisements 'awful' because learning a new language is difficult and takes a long time.

3. **C) Talking to real people and listening to them over a long time** — The speaker says you cannot speak to a cassette; you must talk to people and listen to them, and this takes a long time.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — The script states that many newspapers send their reports to other newspapers in different countries in English, not in Spanish or Italian.

2. **False** — The speaker states that you can only repeat questions and answers with a cassette, and that you cannot learn English from cassettes alone.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **True** — The text states that English is the language of the business world and gives the example of Mexican and Chinese businessmen speaking English together.

2. **True** — In paragraph 2, the writer refers to them as 'those expensive cassettes'.

3. **False** — The writer explicitly says: 'These advertisements are awful.'

4. **False** — The writer is an English teacher who doubts that anyone can learn a language in three months and sarcastically asks whether 'super teachers' teach those classes.

5. **False** — The writer says: 'I don't think people can learn English from cassettes.'

6. **At airports and at the cinema.** — Paragraph 1 says: 'At airports you can always hear English, and at the cinema you can watch a film in English.'

7. **Our friends, our neighbours, or a waiter in a restaurant.** — Paragraph 3 lists these examples: 'we talk to our friends, our neighbours or to a waiter in a restaurant.'


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **B) To show doubt that anyone can really teach a language in three months** — The teacher uses 'super teachers' sarcastically to show disbelief that anyone could teach a whole new language in just three months.

2. **C) Real communication with people over time is needed to learn a language.** — Paragraph 3 emphasizes that real language involves daily communication with real people ('talk to people and listen to them'), not just repeating recorded sentences.

3. **A) Public notices trying to sell a product or service** — The writer refers to ads in newspapers and on buses that try to persuade people to buy courses or cassettes.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (The Truth About Learning English):**

English is important today for international news, business, travel, and films. Because many people want to learn it, advertisements often promise that buyers can master English in three months using expensive cassettes or short courses. However, the writer, an English teacher, disagrees. Real language learning is difficult and takes a long time because learners need to talk and listen to real people every day, not just repeat sentences from a cassette.


**Task 2 (How Should We Learn a New Language?):**

I agree with the teacher that learning a language takes time and real communication. Apps, videos, or recordings can help us learn new words and pronunciation, just like cassettes did in the past. However, we cannot become fluent only by repeating sentences to a screen. To truly learn a language, we need to speak with real people, ask questions, and make mistakes in daily conversations. Regular practice over many months or years is the best way to succeed.


---


## Passage 53: The Worlds of Walt Disney (`raw1_053` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **1923**

2. **400 million / four hundred million**

3. **hotels**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **C) In 1955** — The passage states that Disneyland opened in Anaheim, California in 1955.

2. **B) Mickey Mouse** — According to the speaker, Mickey Mouse was created in 1928, after Disney made 'Alice in Wonderland'.

3. **D) It is still under construction** — The passage explains that people will not move into the second city until 1995 because it is still being built.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — The passage states that Walt Disney died of cancer in 1966, whereas Disney World was finished in 1971.

2. **True** — The passage says that Walt Disney was very interested in technology, so all his work was technically excellent.


### Reading Cevapları


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **B) Walt Disney's career and his two famous entertainment parks** — The passage gives an overview of Walt Disney's early cartoon career and the creation of Disneyland and Disney World.

2. **C) Disney World offers accommodation and shopping in addition to entertainment.** — The passage mentions that Disneyland has four parts (Adventureland, Frontierland, Fantasyland, and Tomorrowland), while Disney World also has hotels and shopping centres.

3. **A) One of the cities is finished, while the other is still being built.** — Because Lake Buena Vista is already completed and people are moving in, while the second city will not be ready until 1995, the two cities are at different stages of completion.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Summary: Walt Disney and His Parks):**

Walt Disney started making cartoon films with his brother Roy in 1923 and created Mickey Mouse in 1928. He wanted to provide clean fun for families, so he opened Disneyland in California in 1955. Later, in 1960, he began building Disney World in Florida, which opened in 1971 with hotels and shopping centres. Although Walt Disney died in 1966, millions of people still remember him when they visit his parks.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: Family Holidays at Theme Parks):**

In my opinion, entertainment parks are wonderful places for family holidays. First, both children and parents can find fun activities to do together, so everyone feels happy and relaxed. Second, parks like Disney World have hotels, restaurants, and shops in one safe area. This makes travelling much easier for families with young children because they do not need to go far to eat or sleep. For these reasons, theme parks create unforgettable memories for the whole family.

## Passage 54: Letter from Family Friends Agency (`raw1_054` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **underground station**

2. **six months / 6 months**

3. **two beds / 2 beds**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **C) It can be too noisy because of the children and dogs.** — The letter states that the Cranleys have five children and three dogs, so some students think it is too noisy.

2. **A) Every student gets a private room with a washbasin and shower.** — The letter says Mrs. Eisley has five bedrooms in her house and two more single bedrooms in another house on the same street, and every bedroom has its own washbasin and shower.

3. **D) The house is quite far from central London.** — The letter mentions that some students do not like Wimbledon because it is quite far from central London.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — The letter says the Cranleys can take up to four students, and Professor and Mrs. Earl-Jones have two bedrooms with two beds each (four students). Mrs. Eisley has five bedrooms plus two single bedrooms in another house, which means seven students.

2. **Not Given** — The passage describes the houses, food, locations, and booking time, but it does not mention the price of staying with any of the families.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **On 17 October.** — The letter begins with 'Thank you for your letter of 17 October.'

2. **With a good family.** — The letter states: 'You asked about good families for students to stay with in London.'

3. **With Mr. and Mrs. Cranley (at Garden Street, London NW1).** — The Cranleys are a large family with five children and three dogs.

4. **Seven students.** — There are five bedrooms for students in Mrs. Eisley's house and two more single bedrooms in another house on the same street, and all students eat with Mrs. Eisley (5 + 2 = 7).

5. **At Mrs. Eisley's house (3 Newton Avenue, Uxbridge).** — At Mrs. Eisley's, everyone has his or her own room and each bedroom has a washbasin and shower.

6. **She sings opera (she is an opera singer).** — The letter states: 'Mr. Jones is a well-known professor, and his wife sings opera.'

7. **Because it is too noisy (they have five children and three dogs).** — The letter explains that some students do not like staying with the Cranleys because it is too noisy.

8. **Because everyone likes Mrs. Eisley, so you must book at least six months in advance.** — The letter states: 'Everyone likes Mrs. Eisley, so it's very difficult to get in. You must book at least six months in advance.'

9. **Because Wimbledon is quite far from central London.** — The letter notes: 'Some students don't like Wimbledon because it's quite far from central London.'

10. **By underground.** — The Cranleys' house is described as being in a good location near an underground station.

11. **Four students.** — There are two bedrooms for students with two beds in each bedroom (2 x 2 = 4).


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **B) To suggest three host families for John Bear in London** — The agency is replying to John Bear's request by offering three homestay family options in London for his university studies.

2. **C) Her rooms are very popular among students.** — Because everyone likes Mrs. Eisley and you must book at least six months in advance, her rooms are in very high demand.

3. **A) reserve a place beforehand** — The phrase 'book at least six months in advance' means reserving a room six months before moving in.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Summary: Comparing Three Homestay Options):**

Family Friends Agency offers John Bear three homestay choices in London. The Cranleys live in a central area near an underground station, but their house with five children and three dogs can be noisy. Mrs. Eisley offers private rooms with showers, though students must book six months early. Finally, Professor and Mrs. Earl-Jones have a lovely house with a large garden and great food, even though Wimbledon is far from central London.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: Host Family vs. Dormitory):**

If I were a university student in London, I would choose to live with a host family rather than in a student dormitory. First, staying with a local family helps international students practise their English every day during meals and conversations. Second, host families usually provide homemade food and a warm, friendly home, so students do not feel lonely in a new city. Although a dormitory gives more independence, a host family offers better comfort and support for a new student.


---


## Passage 55: Letter from Kathy (`raw1_055` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **children**

2. **a hundred years / 100 years**

3. **next month**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **C) It was on the eighth floor and on a busy main road.** — Kathy says she is afraid of high places, and the flat was on the eighth floor and on a busy main road.

2. **B) It was a bit far from town.** — Kathy explains that the country house was a bit far from town, so Peter did not like the idea at first.

3. **D) With help from several friends** — Kathy says several friends have offered to help them move next month.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — Kathy mentions that the flat wasn't far from Peter's office, so he liked it very much, and the children were happy because some friends lived next door.

2. **False** — Kathy saw the advertisement for a really cheap house in the country in the evening paper.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **False** — Peter liked the flat because it was near his office, and the children were happy because some of their friends lived next door.

2. **False** — Kathy saw an advertisement for the cheap country house in the evening paper; friends offered to help them move, not rent them a house.

3. **True** — Kathy writes that there is a very good private school nearby and they plan to send the children there.

4. **a) It was on the eighth floor of a large block of flats, and she is afraid of high places. b) It was also on a busy main road.** — In paragraph 2, Kathy states that the flat was on the eighth floor (and she is afraid of heights) and on a busy main road.

5. **Any two of: It is really cheap; it has four large bedrooms (it is really big); it has a big garden for the children to play in; there is a very good private school nearby.** — Paragraph 3 lists the house's low price, four large bedrooms, big garden, and nearby private school as advantages.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **A) Kathy's family decided to buy an old country house instead of a city flat.** — Kathy explains that the flat in town was on the eighth floor and on a busy main road, whereas she and the children loved the cheap four-bedroom country house with a big garden and nearby school.

2. **C) update the old house so it is modern** — The country house is over a hundred years old, so 'modernise it completely' means updating and repairing it to make it modern and comfortable.

3. **B) He agreed mainly because his family loved the house so much.** — Peter didn't like the country house at first because it was far from town, and he only agreed because Kathy and the children were so enthusiastic.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Summary: Choosing a New Home):**

Kathy and her family need a bigger home because their children are older. First, they found a modern flat in town near Peter's office and the children's friends, but Kathy disliked it because it was on the eighth floor and on a busy road. Later, they visited an old, cheap four-bedroom house in the country. Although it is far from town and needs modernising, it has a big garden and a good school nearby, so they decided to move there next month.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: Town Flat vs. Country House):**

I would prefer to live in a house in the country rather than a flat in town. First, a country house usually has a big garden and much more space, which is wonderful for children and pets. Second, life in the country is quiet and peaceful, with fresh air and less traffic noise than a busy main road. Even though it takes longer to travel to work or shops, living close to nature makes people happier and healthier.


---


## Passage 56: Single Fathers' Club (`raw1_056` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **radio announcer**

2. **30 fathers / thirty fathers**

3. **Tuesday night**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **B) He read a radio announcement for a single mothers' group.** — The passage explains that John McCarthy was reading an announcement for a single mothers' group when he said single fathers needed a group too.

2. **C) Men from many different jobs, as well as students and unemployed men** — The passage lists doctors, students, mechanics, a bus-driver, a television producer, a few businessmen, and several unemployed men.

3. **D) Learning to talk openly about personal topics with other men** — The final sentence states that the difficult part for men is just learning to talk to other men about anything really personal.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — The passage says Henry Mason and Paul Singer heard John McCarthy on the radio and called him, and the three of them started the group together on May 5th, 1988.

2. **Not Given** — The passage mentions that the club started in the US and grew to 30 members after six months, but it does not say whether John McCarthy's group spread to other countries.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **False** — Self-help groups bring together people with many different common problems (such as losing weight, giving up drinking, single mothers, or single fathers) to help each other with their shared issues.

2. **False** — John McCarthy was a radio announcer who simply read an announcement for a single mothers' group on the radio.

3. **False** — The passage states that the group includes students and several men who are unemployed, so not all members work.

4. **True** — The final sentence states that the difficult part for men is just learning to talk to other men about anything really personal.

5. **They started the first single fathers' group in the US ("Single Fathers' Club").** — The passage states: 'These three men started the first single fathers' group in the US on May 5th, 1988.'

6. **Any two of: how to look after a child with a temperature; where to find milk at 6 a.m.; how to help children who don't sleep well at night; what foods are best for their children.** — The final paragraph lists these four specific childcare problems discussed at the meetings.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **C) The creation and purpose of a self-help group for single fathers** — The passage explains what self-help groups are and tells the story of how the first single fathers' club in the US was founded and what members discuss.

2. **B) They focus on practical everyday problems of raising children.** — The passage lists everyday parenting challenges such as caring for a sick child, finding milk early in the morning, sleep problems, and choosing healthy food.

3. **A) alone, without anyone else's help** — Looking after children 'on my own' means taking care of them alone, without a partner or help from another parent.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Summary: The Single Fathers' Club):**

Self-help groups bring together people with shared problems so they can support one another. In 1988, radio announcer John McCarthy, who was raising two sons alone, asked other single fathers on the radio to form a group. Together with Henry Mason and Paul Singer, he started the first Single Fathers' Club in the US. Within six months, 30 fathers with different jobs met every Tuesday night to share practical childcare advice.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: The Value of Self-Help Groups):**

I think self-help groups are very useful for people facing difficult situations in life. First, talking to others who have the same problem makes people feel less alone. When someone listens and truly understands your feelings, it is easier to stay strong. Second, group members can share helpful practical advice from their own experiences, such as how to care for a child or solve daily problems. For these reasons, support groups help people feel better and find real solutions.


---


## Passage 57: Plane Crash in Peru (`raw1_057` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **3,000 / 3000 / three thousand**

2. **sweets**

3. **farmers**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **B) It hit a mountain during a terrible storm.** — The plane hit a mountain forty-five minutes after leaving Lima because of a terrible storm.

2. **C) She heard them, but they could not see each other because of the trees.** — She heard helicopters, but she could not see them above the trees, and they could not see her.

3. **C) Five days** — She came to a river after four days, and walked and swam down it for another five days.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — Juliana and her mother were flying to Pucallpa to spend Christmas with her father, who worked as a bank manager there.

2. **Not Given** — The passage states that three other people survived the crash, but it does not say how or why they failed to get out of the jungle.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **True** — The passage states that they left Lima and were going to Pucallpa, "another town in Peru."

2. **False** — Juliana's father was not on holiday; he was the manager of a bank in Pucallpa.

3. **False** — The plane hit a mountain forty-five minutes after leaving Lima, not a few hours later.

4. **True** — Juliana called for her mother, and nobody answered.

5. **False** — Her left arm was broken and one knee was badly hurt, not a broken leg.

6. **True** — The text says she had no shoes and was wearing only a badly torn dress.

7. **False** — She heard the helicopters, but could not see them above the trees.

8. **True** — She reached a small village house, and that afternoon four farmers arrived there.

9. **False** — Three other people survived the crash, but Juliana was the only one who got out of the jungle.

10. **They were going to Pucallpa to spend Christmas with Juliana's father, who was a bank manager there.** — Paragraph 1 explains that they wanted to spend Christmas with her father, the manager of a bank in Pucallpa.

11. **Because she did not have anything to eat for two days.** — Paragraph 2 states: "She did not have anything to eat for two days, so she felt very weak."

12. **It took her five days.** — Paragraph 3 states: "She walked and swam down the river for another five days. At last she came to a small village house."


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **C) A teenager's remarkable survival after a plane crash** — The passage tells the chronological story of how Juliana survived a plane crash and walked out of the Peruvian jungle over ten days.

2. **B) She realized waiting at the crash site would lead to her death.** — She knew she would die if she stayed at the crash site and could not be seen from above, so following a river was her best way to reach people.

3. **A) Not awake or aware of anything** — In this context, 'unconscious' means knocked out / unaware of her surroundings after hitting the ground.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Guided Summary: Juliana's Survival Story):**

On Christmas Eve in 1971, seventeen-year-old Juliana Koepke survived a plane crash in Peru after falling 3,000 metres in her seat. Although her arm was broken and she had no food or shoes, she decided to walk out of the jungle. After four days she reached a river and followed it for five days until she found a village house, where four farmers rescued her.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: Key to Survival):**

I think Juliana survived mainly because she stayed calm and kept moving. Even though she was badly hurt and had no shoes or food, she understood that waiting in the jungle would not save her life. Walking for days without food takes great courage and determination. Moreover, finding the river was very important because rivers often lead to villages where people live. In difficult situations, thinking clearly and never giving up can help a person survive against all odds.


---


## Passage 58: Inverness (`raw1_058` · `B1`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **1822**

2. **haggis**

3. **80,000 / 80000 / eighty thousand**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **C) By train, plane, and bus** — The passage notes that Inverness has an excellent transport system with trains to Aberdeen, Dundee, Glasgow, and Edinburgh, Dalcross Airport, and local buses.

2. **B) Steamships replaced sailing boats and were too big for the canal.** — Steamships became popular, and they were too large to travel down the canal, so people stopped using it for transport.

3. **A) A courthouse** — Inverness Castle was built in 1834 and is now used as a courthouse.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — Inverness is an old town, and although there are not many old buildings left, there are still very old houses on Douglas Row and old churches on Church Street, plus Abertaff House from 1592.

2. **True** — The passage says that many restaurants and hotels offer meals to non-residents at cheap prices.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **True** — Paragraph 2 mentions that the houses on Douglas Row are very old and Church Street has interesting old churches.

2. **True** — Paragraph 4 recommends trying 'haggis' and a glass of malt whisky before leaving.

3. **False** — The Museum displays a collection about the history of the Highlands, whereas the Art Gallery exhibits paintings, sculptures, and photographs.

4. **d) Transport system and banks.** — Paragraph 1 describes the transport system and banks, and then says: "As well as these essential services, the town has cultural and entertainment facilities."

5. **c) Inverness is at one end of the canal.** — Paragraph 3 states that Inverness is situated at the northern end of the Caledonian Canal.

6. **b) the Scottish language** — The text describes Gaelic as "the language which about 80,000 Scottish people speak."

7. **It is the famous mountainous area in Scotland.** — Paragraph 1 defines the Highlands as "the famous mountainous area in Scotland."

8. **Aberdeen, Dundee, Glasgow, and Edinburgh.** — Paragraph 1 lists train connections from Inverness to Aberdeen, Dundee, Glasgow, and Edinburgh.

9. **b) where Inverness is, d) the canal, e) what to eat and drink, g) interesting places to see** — The text covers Inverness's location (north-east Scotland, northern end of the canal), the Caledonian Canal, local food and drink (haggis, malt whisky, seafood), and Places of Interest. Weather, hotel prices, and the population of Inverness are not mentioned.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **D) To inform visitors about what Inverness offers tourists** — The passage describes Inverness's location, transport, banks, history, canal, food, and places of interest, acting as a tourist guide.

2. **B) Visitors can expect to see different artworks at different times of the year.** — In the Art Gallery, artists exhibit works "which change frequently", meaning visitors can see new exhibitions at different times.

3. **C) Basic, necessary practical services such as transport and banking** — The passage contrasts "essential services" (transport and banks) with "cultural and entertainment facilities", showing that essential services are basic practical needs for travellers.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Summary: Visiting Inverness):**

Inverness is a small yet well-connected town in north-east Scotland that serves as a gateway to the Highlands. Tourists can easily reach it by train, plane, or bus and use essential services like banks. Visitors can explore historic buildings, take sightseeing boat trips along the 1822 Caledonian Canal, enjoy affordable local dishes such as haggis and seafood, and visit cultural landmarks including Inverness Castle, the Museum and Art Gallery, Abertaff House, and St Andrew's Cathedral.


**Task 2 (Opinion Essay: Small Towns vs. Big Cities):**

Small historic towns like Inverness are often more attractive to tourists than large cities because they combine convenience with local character. First, although Inverness is small, it still provides modern transport links and banking services, which makes travelling stress-free. Second, smaller towns allow visitors to experience authentic regional culture, such as traditional Highland food, historic architecture, and local languages like Gaelic, without the crowds of a major metropolis. Finally, being close to natural scenery like the Highlands and the Caledonian Canal offers peaceful sightseeing opportunities. For these reasons, small towns can provide a richer and more relaxing travel experience.


---


## Passage 59: Lacrosse (`raw1_059` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **war**

2. **seventy / 70**

3. **crosse**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **A) Before Columbus arrived in the New World** — Indians in northern New York State and southern Ontario, Canada, invented lacrosse before Columbus arrived.

2. **C) 140 grams** — The passage says the ball is 21 centimetres around and weighs 140 grams.

3. **B) Players catch and pass the ball at high speed with their sticks.** — Lacrosse is fast because players can catch and pass the ball at high speed using their sticks.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — People play lacrosse outdoors on a field that is seventy meters long.

2. **True** — The passage states that lacrosse was once the national summer sport in Canada and is also popular in Britain and Australia.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **Canada, the United States (America), Britain, and Australia.** — The passage mentions Canada, America (northern New York State), Britain, and Australia.

2. **There are two goals, one at each end of the field.** — Paragraph 2 states: "At each end of the field there is a goal."

3. **Each player has a stick called a "crosse".** — Paragraph 2 says: "Each player has a stick called a 'crosse'."

4. **b) Indians** — Paragraph 1 states that the Indians in northern New York State and southern Ontario invented lacrosse.

5. **c) 140 grams** — Paragraph 2 says the ball "weighs 140 grams."

6. **c) Twenty** — There are ten players on each team, and two teams play against each other, making twenty players in total.

7. **c) on a field** — Paragraph 2 says: "People play lacrosse outdoors. The lacrosse field is seventy meters long."

8. **d) a ball with a stick** — Each player has a stick called a "crosse", and the players hit a ball with their sticks.

9. **a) quickly** — The passage states that players "can catch and pass the ball at a high speed", which means quickly.

10. **a) Canada** — Paragraph 3 states: "At one time lacrosse was the national summer sport in Canada."


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **C) To describe the history, rules, and popularity of lacrosse** — Paragraph 1 gives the origin of lacrosse, Paragraph 2 explains the field, equipment, and rules, and Paragraph 3 mentions where it is popular.

2. **A) There are twenty players on the field during a standard game.** — With ten players on each of the two teams, there are twenty players on the field in total.

3. **D) It has existed in North America for many centuries.** — The game existed before Columbus arrived and was used to train warriors, showing it has deep cultural and historical roots in North America.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Guided Summary: What is Lacrosse?):**

Lacrosse is one of the oldest organized sports in North America. Native Americans invented it before Columbus arrived in order to train for war. Today, it is played outdoors on a seventy-meter field with two net goals and ten players on each team. Players use sticks called crosses to catch, pass, and hit a 140-gram ball into the net. Once Canada's national summer sport, it is also popular in Britain and Australia.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: Benefits of Team Sports):**

I think team sports like lacrosse are great for young people because they teach both physical fitness and cooperation. Since lacrosse is a very fast game, players have to run, react quickly, and improve their strength. More importantly, players must pass the ball well and work together with their teammates to score goals. Through team sports, young people also learn how to follow rules, respect their opponents, and make new friends outside the classroom.


---


## Passage 60: The Barasana (`raw1_060` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **15,000 / fifteen thousand**

2. **leaves / the leaves**

3. **father's / fathers**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **C) One hour** — The speaker explains that the houses are very far apart and a person must walk for one hour to get from one house to another.

2. **B) Men must marry women from houses with different languages.** — Because people in different houses speak different languages and a Barasana man must marry a woman from another house, husbands and wives must learn each other's languages.

3. **A) Swimming, fishing, and practising hunting** — According to the passage, young girls have to help their mothers, while boys usually swim in the rivers, go fishing, and practise hunting animals.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — The passage states that the Barasana do not live in villages; instead, many families share one very big house.

2. **Not Given** — The passage mentions that Barasana men go fishing and hunt animals for meat, and that they pick bananas, but it does not say how many hours a day they spend hunting.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **False** — Tukano, Desana, and Cubeo are names of small groups of Amazonian Indians, not cities in Colombia.

2. **False** — About 15,000 Amazonian Indians in total live in the Vaupés Region; the Barasana are one of the groups living there.

3. **True** — The passage states that many families live together in one very big house and share everything.

4. **False** — The men and women do different things: the women do housework, look after children, work in the gardens, and prepare food, while the men go fishing and hunt animals.

5. **False** — The fourth paragraph explicitly says that a Barasana man has to marry a woman from a different house.

6. **True** — The children learn their father's language first and use it every day, and they also understand their mother's language.

7. **False** — Young girls have to help their mothers, but the boys do not.

8. **They live in the Vaupés Region, an area between Colombia and Brazil.** — The first paragraph states that about 15,000 Amazonian Indians live in an area between Colombia and Brazil called 'The Vaupés Region'.

9. **The Barasana.** — The second paragraph says, 'The Barasana have a very different life-style. They do not live in villages.'

10. **The Barasana women.** — According to the third paragraph, the women look after the children, work in the gardens, and prepare the food.

11. **They play with other children and watch their parents.** — The final paragraph explains that the children play with other children and watch their parents, and in this way they learn about life.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **B) The daily life, customs, and family structure of the Barasana people** — The passage describes how the Barasana live in shared houses, divide work between men and women, learn languages through marriage, and raise their children without schools.

2. **C) wonderful** — In the fourth paragraph, the text explains that 'The Barasana are marvellous language-learners' because they must learn their spouse's language. 'Marvellous' means wonderful or very good.

3. **A) Barasana children learn practical skills through daily life rather than formal schooling.** — Because children do not go to school and instead learn by watching their parents and practising adult activities (girls helping mothers, boys fishing and hunting), we can infer that education is informal and based on daily life.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Guided Summary: Life Among the Barasana):**

The Barasana are Amazonian Indians who live in the Vaupés Region between Colombia and Brazil. Instead of living in villages, several families share one large house far from other houses. Women do housework, grow plants, and care for children, while men fish and hunt. Because men marry women from different houses, couples learn each other's languages. Children do not attend school; they learn by watching adults and playing.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: Sharing a Home with Many Families):**

Life in a large shared house has both good and difficult sides. On the positive side, families never feel lonely because many people live together and help each other every day. Sharing food, housework, and child care can make daily tasks easier. However, living with so many people in one house means there is very little privacy. People might also disagree about rules or chores. In addition, Barasana houses are an hour apart, so visiting friends in other houses takes a lot of time. Overall, a shared house creates a strong community, even if life there is less private.


---


## Passage 61: The Motor Car Industry (`raw1_061` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **workshops**

2. **1909**

3. **Model T / Ford Model T**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **B) Cars were too expensive for most people.** — Cars were very expensive because they were built in small workshops where mechanics made many small parts with only a few machines.

2. **C) He opened a factory that used machines to make car parts.** — In 1909, Henry Ford opened a factory with machines that made the parts of the cars, allowing him to produce cheaper cars very fast.

3. **A) Only one kind of car** — The passage states that Henry Ford's factory made only one kind of motor car: the famous Ford Model T.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — The first sentence lists Great Britain, France, Germany, and the United States as the countries where motor cars were made at the end of the 19th century.

2. **False** — In small workshops, mechanics had to make many small parts with the help of only a few machines, not many modern machines.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **b) mechanics made them** — The passage explains that cars were very expensive because they were made in small workshops where mechanics had to make many small parts with only a few machines.

2. **d) made the first Ford "Model T"** — The passage states that Henry Ford opened a factory with machines making car parts, and this factory made only one kind of motor car, the famous Ford "Model T".


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **D) How car production changed from expensive workshops to Ford's factory** — The first paragraph explains why early cars were expensive in workshops, and the second paragraph explains how Henry Ford changed car production by opening a factory in 1909.

2. **A) Using machines to make parts of a single car model lowered costs and increased speed.** — Because Henry Ford's factory used machines to make parts quickly and made only one model, it was able to make cheaper cars much faster than small workshops.

3. **C) widely liked and bought** — The passage explains that cars were not popular for many years because they were too expensive; here 'popular' means widely bought or liked by many people.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Summary: From Workshops to Ford's Factory):**

At the end of the 19th century, motor cars were made in countries like Great Britain, France, Germany, and the United States. Because mechanics made small parts in workshops with few machines, cars were very expensive and unpopular. However, in 1909, an American engineer named Henry Ford opened a factory with machines to make car parts quickly. His factory produced cheaper cars, making only one model: the Ford Model T.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: How Affordable Cars Changed Life):**

Henry Ford's factory changed modern life in many important ways. Before 1909, only very rich people could buy motor cars because they were so expensive. When Ford used machines to build the Model T quickly and cheaply, ordinary families could afford to buy a car for the first time. Cars allowed people to travel longer distances for work, visit family in other towns, and transport goods faster. Although cars also brought traffic and pollution later, Ford's factory helped connect communities and shaped the modern world we live in today.


---


## Passage 62: The Ostrich (`raw1_062` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **small**

2. **40 / forty**

3. **daytime / day**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **C) Africa and Arabia** — The speaker says that Africa and Arabia are the home of the ostrich, though ostriches can also be seen in zoos around the world.

2. **B) A small, flat head and strong, thick legs** — The passage describes the ostrich as having a small, flat head, a very long neck, and strong, thick legs.

3. **D) In holes in the sand** — According to the passage, ostriches leave their eggs in holes in the sand, and sometimes there are 20 eggs in one hole.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — The passage states that ostrich eggs are white and are the largest eggs in the world.

2. **Not Given** — The passage mentions that ostriches eat plants and some insects, and that they live in Africa, Arabia, and zoos, but it does not say how long they live.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **False** — The first sentence states that the ostrich is the largest bird in the world.

2. **False** — Africa and Arabia are the home of the ostrich, even though we can also see ostriches in almost every zoo around the world.

3. **True** — The passage says the ostrich has got very small wings, so it cannot fly.

4. **True** — The ostrich is faster than a horse, and the antelope is faster than the ostrich, which means an antelope is faster than a horse.

5. **True** — The passage states that some male ostriches weigh from 90 to 135 kg, and 125 kg falls within that range.

6. **False** — The passage says the food of the ostrich is plants and some insects; it does not say ostriches eat the eggs of other birds.

7. **False** — The last sentence states that the female ostrich looks after the eggs in the daytime and the male ostrich looks after them at night.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **B) The antelope** — The passage explains that an ostrich can run about 40 miles an hour and is faster than a horse, but the antelope is faster than the ostrich. Therefore, the antelope is the fastest of the three.

2. **C) Both male and female ostriches share the job of protecting their eggs.** — Because both the female and the male ostrich take turns watching the eggs (daytime and night), we can infer that both parents share the responsibility of protecting their eggs.

3. **A) To give basic facts about the ostrich's body, habits, and eggs** — The passage introduces the ostrich's size, appearance, speed, diet, and how it cares for its eggs, giving a general overview of the bird.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Summary: Facts About the Ostrich):**

The ostrich is the world's largest bird and lives in Africa, Arabia, and zoos worldwide. Although it has small wings and cannot fly, its strong legs help it run about 40 miles an hour. Ostriches have small heads, long necks, and eat plants and insects. They lay the largest white eggs in sand holes. The female watches the eggs during the day, and the male watches them at night.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: Survival Without Flying):**

Even though the ostrich cannot fly, it has special features that help it survive in the wild. First, its strong, thick legs allow it to run up to 40 miles an hour, so it can escape from dangerous animals easily. Second, its very long neck helps it see far across open land and spot enemies early. Finally, ostriches eat both plants and insects, which makes finding food easier in dry places. For these reasons, running fast and seeing well are just as useful as flying for an ostrich.


---


## Passage 63: Rudyard Kipling (`raw1_063` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **1865**

2. **reporter**

3. **Nobel Prize**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **B) Because his father sent him there to go to school** — Kipling was born in India, and when he was 5 years old, his father sent him to England to go to school at the United Services College in Devon.

2. **C) Stalky and Co.** — The speaker mentions that Kipling wrote about his school in Devon later in his book called Stalky and Co.

3. **A) Vermont, in the United States** — After marrying Caroline Balstier in 1892, they lived in Vermont in the United States for some time and later returned to Sussex, England.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — The passage mentions that Kipling travelled to India, China, Japan, and America before finally going to England.

2. **False** — The passage states that Kipling became very famous at the age of 25, mainly because of his stories about soldiers, long before he won the Nobel Prize in 1907.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **True** — Kipling was born in Bombay, India, on December 30th, 1865, and stayed there until he was 5 years old (1870), when his father sent him to England.

2. **False** — The passage states that the United Services College is in Devon, England, not in the U.S.A.

3. **False** — He worked as a reporter when he returned to India at age 17, not in Sussex, England.

4. **False** — When he went to England after his travels, he worked on stories of India and wrote Barrack Room Ballads.

5. **True** — The passage says he became very famous at the age of 25, which is in his twenties.

6. **his school (the United Services College in Devon)** — The first paragraph states that he went to school at the United Services College in Devon and wrote about this school later in Stalky and Co.

7. **soldiers** — According to the passage, his stories, especially those about soldiers, were very popular and he became very famous at the age of 25.

8. **wife** — In 1892, Kipling married an American named Caroline Balstier, so she was his wife.

9. **children** — The passage lists The Jungle Book (1894) as one of Kipling's children's stories.

10. **the Nobel Prize for literature** — The last sentence states that Kipling received the 1907 Nobel Prize for literature.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **C) He returned to India to work as a reporter before marrying Caroline Balstier.** — Let's check the order of events: he went to school in Devon (age 5-17), returned to India and became famous (age 25), married Caroline Balstier (1892), and published The Jungle Book (1894). Therefore, he returned to India before he married Caroline Balstier.

2. **B) Kipling's experiences in India played an important role in his writing career.** — Although Kipling was sent to school in England at age 5 and later settled in Sussex, England, he was born in Bombay, returned there at 17 to work as a reporter, and wrote stories of India. This shows India strongly influenced his writing.

3. **D) Kipling's children's stories** — In the second paragraph, 'Some of them' comes right after 'He also wrote children's stories.' Therefore, 'them' refers to Kipling's children's stories.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Summary: The Life of Rudyard Kipling):**

Rudyard Kipling was born in India in 1865 and went to school in England from age 5 to 17. He returned to India, worked as a reporter, and became famous at 25 for his stories about soldiers. Later, he travelled widely, married Caroline Balstier in 1892, and lived in the United States and England. He wrote famous children's books like The Jungle Book and won the Nobel Prize for literature in 1907.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: Why Children's Stories Matter to Everyone):**

Children's stories are important for people of all ages, not just young children. First, stories like The Jungle Book teach timeless lessons about friendship, courage, and kindness in a simple and memorable way. When adults read these books again, they often notice deeper meanings that they missed when they were young. Second, children's stories are fun to share across generations. Parents and grandparents enjoy reading classic tales to their children and talking about the characters together. For these reasons, great children's books stay meaningful throughout a person's whole life.


---


## Passage 64: Video Clips (`raw1_064` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **first video**

2. **shops / stores**

3. **hundreds**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **C) Over 800,000 dollars** — The speaker states that Michael Jackson had to spend over 800,000 dollars to make the twenty-minute video 'Thriller'.

2. **B) British record companies** — British record companies are spending more than 12 million pounds a year on videos of new records.

3. **A) British videos are much better than American ones.** — Jools Holland says that British film directors' videos are very good and much better than the American ones.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — The passage states that unknown singers and groups can become rich in one night when their pop videos get on MTV.

2. **False** — Jools Holland says that most pop singers are very bad actors, though they do not look bad when companies spend thousands of pounds on the videos.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **c) is known all over the world** — The passage states that 'Thriller' became very famous all over the world (it cost over $800,000 and is twenty minutes long).

2. **b) became well-known with their first video** — The passage states that the Birmingham group 'Duran Duran' became famous only after their first video.

3. **b) people watch their videos on TV** — The passage explains that with videos, singers and groups get on TV and people buy them in shops, and these two things make them famous (they cannot become famous if they do not make a good video).

4. **d) singers and groups** — In the previous sentences, the subject is "singers and groups" ("These two things make singers and groups famous. They can't become famous...").

5. **c) He directs pop music programmes for the MTV.** — The passage identifies Jools Holland as the director of a British TV pop music programme, not an MTV director.

6. **d) most pop singers** — Jools says "most pop singers are very bad actors... but they don't look bad," so "they" refers to most pop singers.

7. **True** — The passage states that unknown singers and groups can become rich in one night when their pop videos get on the American MTV channel.

8. **True** — The passage states that videos became the biggest thing in the pop music world in the 1960s (just over twenty years before this text was published).

9. **True** — The passage says British record companies spend more than £12 million a year on videos of new records.

10. **True** — Jools Holland states that British videos are "much better than the American ones."

11. **False** — Some videos cost more than £50,000 for three minutes of film, while others from new groups cost £500 or less.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **B) The importance and production of pop music videos** — The passage explains how music videos help singers and groups become famous, how much money companies spend on them, and how they are produced at different budgets.

2. **C) Skilled directors and large budgets hide their poor acting.** — The passage notes that singers look good even though they are bad actors because record companies spend thousands of pounds and hire excellent film directors.

3. **D) The cost of making a pop video varies widely depending on the artist.** — By contrasting videos costing over £50,000 for three minutes with ones costing £500 or less from new groups, the author shows that video budgets vary greatly.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Guided Summary: The Role of Music Videos):**

Pop music videos play a major role in helping singers and bands become famous. For example, Michael Jackson spent over $800,000 on 'Thriller', and Duran Duran became well-known after their first video. British record companies spend over £12 million every year on videos so artists can appear on TV and sell videos in shops. Although many singers are poor actors, talented film directors and big budgets make their videos look impressive.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: Music Videos Today):**

In my opinion, music videos are still very important for singers today, even though people watch them on the internet instead of only on TV. A good video helps listeners remember a song and understand the artist's style. For example, a creative video can quickly go viral and bring millions of new fans to an unknown musician. However, a singer does not always need a lot of money to make a good video. With a smartphone and an original idea, new artists can still make great videos and become successful.


---


## Passage 65: Dreams (`raw1_065` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **four / 4**

2. **half**

3. **answers / the answers**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **C) About every 90 minutes** — The speaker explains that sleepers enter the REM stage about every 90 minutes.

2. **B) They felt bad, worried a lot, and got angry easily.** — When people took sleeping pills and could not dream for a few nights, they felt bad, got angry easily, worried a lot, and wanted to fight with everyone.

3. **D) They dreamed all night for a few nights to catch up.** — After stopping the pills, they all began to dream all night for a few nights in order to catch up.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — The first dream of the night lasts about ten minutes, and each dream gets longer until the last dream may be an hour long.

2. **True** — If people expect difficult problems the next day, they may spend more time in REM sleep the night before to look for answers.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **d) the people who took the sleeping pills** — In the previous sentence, doctors gave "some people" sleeping pills, so "them" refers to the people who took the sleeping pills.

2. **c) 60 minutes** — The passage says the last dream of the night may be an hour (60 minutes) long.

3. **d) Six times.** — The first sentence states that people dream four to six times a night.

4. **c) Babies.** — The passage says younger children spend more time dreaming and babies spend almost half of their sleep in the REM stage.

5. **d) All of the above.** — After a few nights without dreams, they began to feel bad, became angry easily, worried a lot, and wanted to fight with everyone.

6. **a) We dream to find answers to our problems.** — The last paragraph explains that dreams give people time to find answers to some of their problems.

7. **False** — The passage states that during REM sleep, you breathe faster and your heart beats faster than in stages one through four.

8. **True** — In the experiment, the sleeping pills prevented people from going into REM sleep, leaving them without dreams.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **A) To explain the REM sleep stage and why dreaming is necessary** — The passage explains what happens physically during REM sleep (breathing and heart rate), how long dreams last, why humans need to dream, and how dreams help solve problems.

2. **C) Missing dreams negatively affects people's mood and behavior.** — Because the people became angry, worried, and wanted to fight after missing REM sleep—and then dreamed all night to recover—the experiment shows dreaming is essential for emotional balance and mental health.

3. **B) to make up for lost dreaming time** — After missing several nights of dreams, they dreamed all night for a few nights "to catch up," meaning to make up for the dreaming time they had lost.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Guided Summary: Why We Dream):**

People usually dream four to six times every night during the REM stage of sleep, when breathing and heartbeats become faster. Each dream gets longer, and babies spend nearly half of their sleep dreaming. An experiment proved that dreams are necessary: when sleeping pills stopped people from dreaming, they became worried and angry. Moreover, dreams can help us find solutions to difficult problems we expect the next day.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: The Value of Good Sleep):**

Good sleep is very important for my daily life. When I do not get enough sleep, I feel tired and find it hard to concentrate at school or work. I also become impatient and worry more easily about small problems. On the other hand, after a full night of deep sleep and dreams, my mind feels fresh and clear. Sometimes I even wake up with a good idea for a problem I could not solve the day before. For this reason, I try to sleep at regular hours every night.


---


## Passage 66: Oranges (`raw1_066` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **twenty / 20**

2. **China**

3. **Florida**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **C) Small white flowers that smell very sweet** — The passage states that an orange tree has small white flowers that smell very sweet, and it has flowers and fruit at the same time.

2. **A) From farmers in Asia and the Middle East** — Farmers in other parts of Asia and the Middle East learned from the Chinese, and then they taught the Europeans.

3. **B) Because the fruit has a very beautiful colour** — The passage explains that the colour of oranges is very beautiful, so English uses the name of the fruit for the colour.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — Early oranges twenty million years ago were very small, not like the oranges today.

2. **True** — The passage says the Chinese started to grow orange trees around 2400 B.C. and Chinese art has lovely old pictures of oranges and orange trees.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **a) Clue: 'that is, separate parts' | b) separate parts (of an orange)** — The phrase "that is, separate parts" immediately defines "sections".

2. **a) Clue: 'i.e., parts which grow into a new plant' | b) parts which grow into a new plant** — The abbreviation "i.e." introduces the definition "parts which grow into a new plant".

3. **a) Clue: 'In other words, these oranges grow in nature' | b) They grow in nature.** — "In other words, these oranges grow in nature" explains what "wild" means.

4. **a) Clue: 'or grow' | b) grow** — The phrase ", or grow," directly defines "raise".

5. **a) Clue: 'such as India and Pakistan' | b) (in) other parts of Asia** — "Such as" introduces India and Pakistan as examples of other parts of Asia.

6. **a) Clue: 'North and South America, called' | b) North and South America** — "North and South America, called the New World" defines "the New World".

7. **a) Clue: '(farm product)' | b) farm product** — The definition "(farm product)" in parentheses explains the word "crop".

8. **c) oranges** — The subject of the previous sentence is "oranges" ("They are in sections... so it is easy to eat them").

9. **the orange tree** — The first sentence introduces "The orange tree", and "It" refers back to the orange tree.

10. **oranges** — In "The oranges were very small, not like the ones today," "ones" replaces "oranges".

11. **China** — The previous sentence ends with "China", so "there" means "in China".

12. **They are sweet and juicy; they are in sections; some do not have seeds (or some have a thick skin and others have a thin skin).** — The first paragraph lists these characteristics of oranges.

13. **The Spanish planted orange trees in North and South America and took them to Florida first.** — Paragraph 4 states that the Spanish planted orange trees in the New World and took them to Florida first.

14. **Because the colour of oranges is very beautiful.** — The final paragraph states that the colour of oranges is very beautiful, so English uses the fruit's name for the colour.

15. **c) seeds** — Paragraph 1 states that some oranges do not have any seeds.

16. **b) Its flowers come after its fruit.** — Paragraph 2 states that an orange tree has flowers and fruit at the same time, so the flowers do not come after the fruit.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **C) The characteristics, history, and name of the orange** — The passage describes what oranges and orange trees look like, traces their history from ancient China to Europe and the Americas, and explains the origin of the colour name.

2. **B) China -> Asia & Middle East -> Europe -> Americas** — The passage gives the chain: China -> other parts of Asia (India, Pakistan) and the Middle East -> Europeans -> Spanish planted them in North and South America (starting in Florida).

3. **D) Oranges have been valued in China for thousands of years.** — The passage notes that the Chinese started raising orange trees around 2400 B.C. and that Chinese art has lovely old pictures of them, showing long-standing cultural and agricultural importance.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Guided Summary: The Story of Oranges):**

Oranges are sweet, juicy fruits divided into easy-to-eat sections, and some have no seeds. Orange trees existed twenty million years ago and probably originated in China, where farmers began growing them around 2400 B.C. From China, orange farming spread to other parts of Asia, the Middle East, and Europe. Later, the Spanish brought orange trees to Florida and the Americas. Today, oranges are an important crop, and English also uses the fruit's name for the colour.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: My Favourite Fruit):**

My favourite fruit is the orange, especially in winter. Oranges are sweet, juicy, and very healthy because they have a lot of vitamin C. I enjoy eating them fresh in sections, and I also like drinking fresh orange juice for breakfast. In addition, orange peel smells wonderful and is sometimes used in cakes and tea. Eating oranges helps me stay healthy during cold weather, so I always keep some in my kitchen.


---


## Passage 67: The Postage Stamp (`raw1_067` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **gum**

2. **Universal Postal System / UPS**

3. **Switzerland**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **B) Both the sender and the receiver had to pay separately** — Before the postage stamp, sending a letter abroad was difficult because the sender paid for part of the trip and the receiver paid for the rest.
