2. **True** — The passage explains that acid rain can damage plants and trees and contaminate drinking water, making it dangerous to human health.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **a) although acid rain is a serious problem for the environment, governments have still not taken the necessary steps to reduce it** — The passage describes the severe environmental and health impacts of acid rain in paragraph 1 and explains why governments avoid passing laws to reduce it in paragraphs 2 and 3.

2. **True** — Paragraph 1 states that smoke from coal-burning power plants contains acidic substances that fall back to earth and contaminate lakes and rivers.

3. **False** — It is governments—not environmentalists—that say the causes of acid rain are not completely clear; environmentalists believe economics is the real reason for inaction.

4. **True** — The last sentence states that it will be extremely expensive to develop the special equipment needed to reduce pollution from these power plants.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **B) To contrast governments' stated excuse with what environmentalists believe is the real motive** — The second paragraph explains that governments blame scientific uncertainty, while environmentalists believe the true reason for inaction is economic cost.

2. **D) Power companies and governments would face very high expenses** — Since most power plants use coal and developing pollution-reducing equipment is extremely expensive, passing strict laws would impose heavy costs on industry and the economy.

3. **C) polluted** — In "contaminated by pollution from power plants", 'contaminated' means polluted or made impure and harmful.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Summary: Effects of Acid Rain and Government Inaction):**

Acid rain, mainly caused by coal-burning power plants, harms aquatic life, trees, plants, and even drinking water, posing a threat to human health. Despite these serious effects, many governments hesitate to pass anti-pollution laws, claiming that the causes are not yet fully understood. However, environmentalists argue that the true obstacle is financial, because developing equipment to cut emissions from coal plants in industrial countries is extremely costly.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: Cost vs. Environmental Protection):**

Although reducing pollution from coal-burning power plants is expensive, governments should prioritize environmental protection over short-term economic savings. Acid rain not only kills fish and destroys forests, but also contaminates drinking water, which directly threatens human health. Ignoring these dangers today will create much higher healthcare and environmental cleanup costs in the future. By passing stricter laws and investing in cleaner equipment now, industrial countries can protect both their citizens and natural resources for future generations.


---


## Passage 100: The Kon-Tiki Voyage, 1947 (`raw1_100` · `A2`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **stone**

2. **four / 4**

3. **102 / one hundred and two**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **B) People knew ancient Peruvians only had wooden rafts** — People did not believe Heyerdahl because they knew the ancient Peruvians only had wooden rafts to sail on.

2. **C) Bad weather and sharks** — During the journey, the explorers faced many problems with the weather and sharks, though the raft did not sink.

3. **A) An ancient god** — They named the raft Kon-Tiki after an ancient god.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **False** — The passage explicitly states that Heyerdahl was a Norwegian explorer.

2. **True** — The passage notes that they spent a long time planning their journey and deciding what to take with them.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **c) the ancient roads and buildings** — "These" at the start of the third sentence refers directly back to "ancient roads and buildings" in the preceding sentence.

2. **d) the giant heads made of stone** — In "Some of them are as tall as a four-storey building," 'them' refers to the "giant heads made of stone" mentioned just before.

3. **a) the rafts** — In "build a raft like those of the ancient Peruvians," 'those' replaces the plural noun "rafts".

4. **c) sailing from Peru to the Polynesian Islands on a raft** — Heyerdahl's voyage demonstrated that sailing across the Pacific from Peru to Polynesia on a wooden raft was indeed possible.

5. **a) is in the Pacific Ocean** — Easter Island is one of the Polynesian Islands, which lie in the middle of the Pacific Ocean.

6. **b) they have found ancient roads and buildings on these islands** — The first paragraph states that ancient roads and buildings on some of the islands show there have been ancient civilizations there.

7. **d) travelled there to start a new life** — Paragraph 2 states Heyerdahl believed people from Ancient Peru sailed across the Pacific "to start a new life on the islands of Polynesia."

8. **a) an ancient god** — Paragraph 3 states: "they called it Kon-Tiki, the name of an ancient god."

9. **b) the Peru Current carried the raft west towards the islands** — Paragraph 4 explains that the natural movement of the water, the Peru Current, carried the raft west to one of the Polynesian Islands.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **C) To prove that ancient Peruvians could have sailed to Polynesia on rafts** — Before modern big ships existed, people wondered how ancient travellers could cross thousands of miles of open ocean; Heyerdahl built a raft like theirs to prove the crossing was possible.

2. **A) determined and adventurous** — Building the raft and sailing 102 days across the Pacific required great determination in the face of scepticism and danger.

3. **D) a steady flow of water** — In the sentence, "current" is explicitly defined as "the natural movement of the water".


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Guided Summary: The Kon-Tiki Expedition):**

People wondered how ancient settlers reached the remote islands of Polynesia without big ships. Norwegian explorer Thor Heyerdahl believed they had sailed over 6,000 kilometres on wooden rafts from ancient Peru. Because nobody believed his theory, he and four friends built a similar raft, named it Kon-Tiki, and set off across the Pacific. Carried west by the Peru Current, they survived storms and sharks and reached Polynesia after 102 days, proving the journey was possible.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: Would You Join an Expedition?):**

I would find it exciting to take part in an expedition like the Kon-Tiki voyage, though it would also be frightening. Sailing across a huge ocean on a simple wooden raft means facing dangerous storms, rough waves, and sharks for months. However, testing an unusual idea in real life and exploring remote islands would be an unforgettable adventure. With careful planning and loyal teammates, as Heyerdahl had, I would love to experience that kind of discovery at least once in my life.


---


## Passage 101: U.S. Cities: Past and Present (`raw1_101` · `B1`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **horses**

2. **communicable**

3. **pickpockets / Pickpockets**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **B) They knew water was polluted, but not how the pollution happened.** — The speaker states that city governments knew water was polluted, 'but they didn't know how it was polluted. Trying to get pure water was a big problem.'

2. **C) They cared only about earning more money.** — According to the passage, real estate developers were 'only interested in earning more money' instead of doing their real job of building well-planned cities with durable housing and attractive neighbourhoods.

3. **A) Runaway horses out of control** — The speaker mentions that while people were not killed by cars back then, 'they were killed or seriously injured by runaway horses, which were running around out of control.'


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — The passage explains that 19th-century cities were actually dirtier than today and that the number of children who died from communicable diseases was high.

2. **False** — In the final paragraph, the speaker says we must look back at the past and learn from it 'so that developments can be made,' implying improvement is possible.


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **horses and other animals (or the remains of killed animals and waste water from kitchens and bathrooms)** — Paragraph 1 mentions 'a lot of pollution from horses and other animals,' and Paragraph 2 adds that butchers left animal remains here and there while sewer systems for waste water from kitchens and bathrooms almost did not exist.

2. **butchers** — Paragraph 2 states: 'Butchers were careless about the cows and pigs which they killed...'

3. **poor planning, crime (or accidents)** — Paragraph 5 states: 'Some of the problems that disturbed people who lived in the cities during that period are still with us - poor planning, crime and accidents.'

4. **water supplies** — Paragraph 3 states: 'Pollution was a very important problem for city governments in the last century, especially the problem of water supplies.'

5. **pickpockets** — Paragraph 6 states: 'Pickpockets, who were very skilled, were able to quickly steal the purses and wallets of the people and run away with them.'

6. **the planning of cities / their neighbourhoods** — Paragraph 5 states: 'The planning of cities and their neighbourhoods was controlled by real estate developers...'

7. **a) animals are killed** — Paragraph 2 explains that cities had their own slaughterhouses where butchers killed cows and pigs.

8. **a) diseases which one person can easily get from another person** — The passage notes that when one of these diseases (like smallpox, cholera, or typhoid) appeared, the situation became dangerous for everyone in that area because they spread easily between people.

9. **c) are no longer under the control of their owners** — The sentence defines runaway horses directly as horses 'which were running around out of control.'

10. **c) American cities were worse in the past than they are now** — The first sentence states that current urban problems are bad, 'but they were worse in the so-called good old days.'

11. **a) crime is still a big problem** — Earlier the passage notes crime is 'still with us,' and adding 'then, too' shows crime was a big problem in the past just as it continues to be today.

12. **c) things can get better** — The writer concludes that we must look back at the past and learn from it 'so that developments can be made.'

13. **They were killed or seriously injured by runaway horses running around out of control.** — Paragraph 7 states that people were killed or seriously injured by runaway horses running around out of control.

14. **Because the cities were very dirty, which led to dangerous communicable diseases such as smallpox, yellow fever, malaria, cholera, and typhoid.** — Paragraph 4 explains that dirt caused communicable diseases like smallpox, yellow fever, malaria, cholera, and typhoid, and as a result, the number of children who died was high.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **C) To show that urban problems existed in an even worse form in the past and offer lessons for today** — Throughout the passage, the author contrasts romanticized views of the 'good old days' with harsh realities like animal waste, lack of sewers, deadly diseases, pickpockets, and runaway horses, concluding we must learn from the past to make improvements.

2. **B) People in the 19th century did not yet understand how waste and dirt contaminated drinking water.** — The passage states that city governments knew water was polluted 'but they didn't know how it was polluted' while also mentioning virtually nonexistent sewer systems and animal remains left everywhere, implying a lack of scientific knowledge about how waste contaminated water.

3. **D) long-lasting and strong** — The passage contrasts real estate developers' greed with what their real job should have been: providing 'both durable housing and a good-looking neighbourhood,' meaning long-lasting, sturdy homes.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Summary: Life in 19th-Century American Cities):**

Although many people remember the past fondly, 19th-century American cities actually faced worse problems than modern cities. Streets and water supplies were heavily polluted by animals, slaughterhouse remains, and a lack of proper sewers, which caused deadly communicable diseases and high child mortality. Meanwhile, greedy real estate developers neglected city planning, pickpockets threatened residents, and runaway horses caused fatal street accidents. Because many of these issues still exist today, we must learn from the past to improve urban life.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: The Most Urgent Urban Problem):**

In my opinion, the most urgent problem in big cities today is poor urban planning, because it directly leads to other serious issues. When cities grow rapidly without thoughtful design, streets become overcrowded, green spaces disappear, and traffic pollution increases. Just as 19th-century developers focused only on making money instead of building durable and attractive neighbourhoods, modern construction sometimes ignores public needs. To make cities healthier and safer, local governments should prioritize long-term planning, reliable public transportation, and clean infrastructure over quick profits.


---


## Passage 102: Spas: Good or Bad? (`raw1_102` · `B1`)

### Listening Cevapları

**A. Gap-Fill:**

1. **mineral springs**

2. **national health system**

3. **casino**


**B. Listening MCQ:**

1. **C) Abano Terme in Italy** — The speaker says Abano Terme in Italy 'offers health-giving radioactive mud to its guests.'

2. **B) They enter a mountain tunnel by truck and lie down naked in hot vapours.** — In the Badgastein 'health tunnel,' visitors drive in by truck wearing a helmet, undress in the depths of the tunnel, and lie down naked surrounded by rich, hot vapours that make them sweat.

3. **D) A short stay at a spa cannot make you healthy if your overall lifestyle is unhealthy.** — One doctor reported that it is silly to expect to become healthy in one or two weeks at a spa if you have an unhealthy lifestyle.


**C. True / False / Not Given:**

1. **True** — The passage begins by stating that water 'is the oldest treatment in medical history' and has been used since the earliest days to soothe and heal the body.

2. **False** — The speaker says that visitors who have tried the Badgastein tunnel 'are very pleased and say that spa treatments are very relaxing.'


### Reading Cevapları

**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular:**

1. **False** — Paragraph 1 states that water 'is the oldest treatment in medical history' and 'has been used for its healing properties since the earliest days.'

2. **False** — Paragraph 2 states that spas 'are very famous in Europe' and attract 'millions of visitors each year.'

3. **False** — According to Paragraph 2, it is Abano Terme in Italy—not Baden Bein Wein in Austria—that offers radioactive mud to its guests.

4. **No Information** — While the passage mentions that German, French, and Austrian national health systems pay for visitors' spa expenses, it does not state that government funding is the reason many people choose to go to spas.

5. **True** — Paragraph 3 explains that because of the heat in the Badgastein tunnel, a person sweats, loses calories, 'and thus decrease[s] in weight.'

6. **No Information** — The British banker only says 'There is usually a casino nearby,' without specifying Baden Baden in Germany.

7. **False** — Paragraph 6 states: 'Not all experts, however, think spas are helpful,' and cites a doctor who criticizes relying on spas without a healthy lifestyle.

8. **c) make feel better** — Paragraph 1 pairs 'to soothe and heal' with 'so that their ill bodies feel better and also more relaxed,' showing that 'soothe' means to make feel better.

9. **d) a place where people go to feel better with the help of water and mud** — Paragraph 2 describes spas as places 'which use water and mud to heal people' where visitors go to relieve aches and smooth their skin.

10. **c) without clothes** — The sentence explicitly says 'you take off your clothes and lie down naked,' meaning without clothes.

11. **b) rewarding** — The next sentence clarifies 'beneficial' as 'do people really get something good and useful for their money?', which matches 'rewarding'.

12. **Water** — 'It' is the subject of the second sentence and refers back to 'Water' in the opening sentence.

13. **people (spa visitors)** — In "do people really get something good and useful for their money?", 'their' (printed as 'there' in the original exercise) refers back to 'people'.

14. **people** — Following 'do people really get something good and useful for their money?', 'Many governments think they do' means many governments think people do get something good and useful for their money.

15. **these governments (German, French, and Austrian governments)** — In 'these governments believe in the healing factor of spas or they wouldn't let the health services pay so much money,' 'they' refers to 'these governments.'

16. **Water (mineral springs) and mud.** — Paragraph 2 states that spas 'use water and mud to heal people,' including mineral springs, hot mud, and radioactive mud.

17. **So that their backs, arms, and legs will stop aching, or their rough skin will feel smooth again.** — Paragraph 2 states that visitors drink and bathe in mineral springs 'so that their backs, arms and legs will stop aching or their rough skin will feel smooth again.'

18. **It has an old miner's tunnel, now used as a 'health tunnel', where visitors drive into the mountain in a truck and lie down naked in rich, hot vapours.** — Paragraph 3 explains that Badgastein has an old miner's tunnel turned into a 'health tunnel' where guests drive into the mountain in a truck wearing a helmet and lie down in hot vapours.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları:**

1. **B) To examine both the claimed health benefits of spas and the criticisms against them** — The author presents the benefits and popularity of spas, strange treatments like Badgastein, government support through national health systems, and opposing views from a doctor and a banker, giving a balanced look at whether spas are good or bad.

2. **C) Governments would not fund spa visits unless they believed spas have real medical value.** — Paragraph 5 argues that German, French, and Austrian governments pay for spa expenses via their national health systems, and adds: 'Obviously, these governments believe in the healing factor of spas or they wouldn't let the health services pay so much money.'

3. **A) similar gambling games** — The phrase 'card games and the like' follows 'lose a lot of money,' referring to card games and similar gambling activities found at a casino.


### Writing Model Cevapları

**Task 1 (Summary: The Debate Over Spa Resorts):**

Spas have been popular in Europe for healing body aches and skin problems using mineral water and mud, and some resorts like Badgastein even offer heated mountain tunnels for weight loss and relaxation. Several European governments support spas through their national health systems. However, critics question their real value: a doctor warned that a short spa stay cannot fix an unhealthy lifestyle, and a banker noted that nearby casinos can cause visitors to gamble away their money.


**Task 2 (Opinion Paragraph: Spas vs. a Healthy Lifestyle):**

In my opinion, spa resorts can be relaxing and helpful for temporary relief, but they are not a substitute for a healthy daily lifestyle. While soaking in warm mineral water or enjoying a mud bath can reduce stress and ease muscle pain, lasting health depends on regular exercise, balanced nutrition, and good sleep every day. As the doctor in the passage pointed out, spending one or two weeks at a spa cannot undo months of poor habits. Therefore, governments and individuals should view spas as a supportive therapy rather than a quick cure.


---
